projected-Gaussian calibration handles the exchange diagonal.  These tools do
not by themselves assert a studentized interval.  They are standard
simulation-output devices, used here only after the killed-leakage renewal gap
law has verified the required exponential tail
\cite{GlynnIglehart1990STS,AsmussenGlynn2007,
VatsFlegalJones2019Multivariate}.

The diagonal statistic below is not a likelihood-ratio statistic and the
feasible half-space is not estimated as a nuisance cone of a parametric
likelihood.  Its cone enters after the visible inclusion CLT is pushed through
the quotient inverse and then projected onto the local hypograph
\(\{h_2\le zh_1\}\).  Thus Shapiro metric-projection differentiability,
Geyer constrained-estimation limits, and Fang--Santos directional-delta
inference are the direct tools used in the proof
\cite{Shapiro1987MetricProjection,Geyer1994ConstrainedM,
FangSantos2019DirectionalDifferentiability}.  The classical boundary
likelihood-ratio theory of Chernoff and Self--Liang is still the relevant
nearby benchmark for cone inference and for the rank-one
\(\tfrac12\delta_0+\tfrac12\chi^2_1\) mixture, but here that mixture arises
from the retained projected-Gaussian quadratic form rather than from a
parametric cone LRT
\cite{Chernoff1954LikelihoodRatio,SelfLiang1987NonstandardLRT}.

The covariance estimator below should be read in this regenerative sense, not
as an MCMC theorem imported wholesale.  MCMC batch-means papers supply useful
terminology for long-run covariance estimation and retained-rank numerical
reporting, but the proof here uses the observed click cycles as regeneration
blocks.  Once local calendar observables are rewritten as cycle rewards,
separated batches are independent at distance one because the reward sequence
is \(1\)-dependent; the rate proof then reduces to endpoint deletion,
calendar-stopped random-prefix replacement, empirical-mean subtraction, and
plug-in centering for those regenerative rewards.

\begin{definition}[Cycle-index inclusion estimator]\label{def:cycle-index-inclusion-estimator-main}
For a finite observed record \(A_0,\ldots,A_{N-1}\), write the complete click
epochs in the observed window as
\[
\tau_0<\tau_1<\cdots<\tau_{K_N},\qquad K_N=\max\{k:\tau_k<N\}.
\]
If the observed window contains no click, set \(K_N=-1\) and every
all-complete-cycle index set below to the empty set.  Thus \(K_N+1\) is the
number of observed clicks, and \(K_N\) is one less than the number of click
epochs from which complete observed cycles can be formed.  Set
\(L_j=\tau_{j+1}-\tau_j\) whenever both endpoints are observed.  For
\(m=0,1,2\), put
\[
X_n^{(m)}=A_nA_{n+m},\qquad X_n^{(0)}=A_n,
\]
and define the complete-cycle uncentered reward
\[
U_j^{(m)}=\sum_{n=\tau_j}^{\tau_{j+1}-1}X_n^{(m)}.
\]
Set \(c_0=c_1=0\) and \(c_2=1\).  The admissible all-complete-cycle index set is
\[
\begin{aligned}
J_{m,N}
=\{0\le j\le K_N-c_m-1:\;&
\tau_j,\tau_{j+1}\in[0,N-1],\\
&\text{and every }n+m\text{ entering }U_j^{(m)}
\text{ lies in }[0,N-1]\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The cycle-index estimator of the three inclusion coordinates
\[
r=(r_0,r_1,r_2)=(\PP(A_0=1),\PP(A_0=A_1=1),\PP(A_0=A_2=1))
\]
is
\[
\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}
=\bigl(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}},
\widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}},
\widehat r_{2,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\bigr)^\top,\qquad
\widehat r_{m,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
=\frac1N\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}U_j^{(m)} .
\]
The displayed sum is an operational estimator only after the relevant
complete-cycle set is nonempty.  Records with no click, too few complete
cycles, or no admissible terminal look-ahead cycles are not assigned the
numerical value \(0\) as an inferential estimate; the finite-record protocol
below instead stops with the message ``insufficient complete cycles; no
confidence set.''  In asymptotic proofs only, we use the harmless auxiliary
extension in which an empty sum is interpreted as \(0\); that extension is not
part of the finite-record report.
For covariance estimation the three coordinates are evaluated on the common
complete-cycle set \(J_N=J_{0,N}\cap J_{1,N}\cap J_{2,N}\).  With plug-in
centering, the vector reward used in the separated batches is
\[
\widehat Y_j=
\bigl(U_j^{(0)}-\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}L_j,\,
U_j^{(1)}-\widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}L_j,\,
U_j^{(2)}-\widehat r_{2,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}L_j\bigr)^\top .
\]
\end{definition}

\paragraph{Reusable regenerative lemmas.}
The local-window-to-cycle decomposition, the generic complete-cycle
regenerative CLT, the calendar-stopped batch covariance rate, the fixed
low-cycle bound, and the uniform block-average estimate are stated and proved
in \Cref{app:finite-record-regenerative-tools-main}.  The main text uses them
only after the D-MAP reward identities below have reduced the inclusion
coordinates to adjacent-gap rewards.

\paragraph{Inclusion-coordinate covariance estimation.}
Algorithm~1 reports \(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}\) only on the retained reporting
event; it does not use a zero matrix as an operational replacement.  Whenever
the main text writes an \(O_{\PP}\) or \(o_{\PP}\) statement for a covariance
matrix as a random element on the full probability space, the object is instead
the auxiliary completed matrix
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}
:=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N}\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
+\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{3\times3}.
\]
This completion is only proof notation; on \(\mathcal E_N^c\) the finite-record
procedure withholds the covariance report and all confidence sets.  The
reusable appendix theorem
\Cref{lem:separated-batch-covariance-estimator-main} is used only through the
following explicit D-MAP specialization, where the random calendar stop,
endpoint/look-ahead deletion, empirical-mean subtraction, and plug-in centering
are kept in the same error decomposition as the reported estimator.

\begin{proposition}[Exact inclusion-coordinate separated-batch covariance rate]\label{prop:exact-inclusion-separated-batch-covariance-main}
Work under \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main} and the deterministic
threshold protocol \ref{fr:protocol-main} at a population point in the stable
quotient chart \(r_0>0\), \(D=r_1-r_0^2\ne0\).  Let
\[
U_j=
\bigl(1,\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},
\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0\}}\bigr)^\top,
\qquad
L_j=G_j+1,
\]
and put \(Y_j=U_j-rL_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\), where
\(r=(r_0,r_1,r_2)^\top\) and \(r_m=\PP(A_0=A_m=1)\).  Then
\[
\EE Y_j=0,\qquad \EE\|Y_j\|^4<\infty .
\]
Let
\[
T_1^{(r)}
=\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)
 +\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0),
\qquad
\Sigma_r=T_1^{(r)}/\mu,\qquad \mu=\EE L_0 .
\]
Choose a deterministic polynomial batch length \(b_N=\lfloor N^\beta\rfloor\),
\(0<\beta<1\), set \(d_N=b_N+1\), and use the separated cycle-index blocks
\[
I_{\ell,N}=\{(\ell-1)d_N,\ldots,(\ell-1)d_N+b_N-1\}.
\]
Let \(J_N=J_{0,N}\cap J_{1,N}\cap J_{2,N}\), let \(M_N\) be the largest
integer such that \(I_{\ell,N}\subseteq J_N\) for
\(1\le\ell\le M_N\), and write \(K_N=\max\{k:\tau_k<N\}\), with
\(K_N=-1\) if the retained word has no click.  On \(M_N\ge2\), define
\[
B_{\ell,N}=\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}Y_j,\qquad
\bar B_N=M_N^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}B_{\ell,N},
\]
and the infeasible true-centered covariance
\[
\widetilde\Sigma_{r,N}
=
\frac{K_N}{N}\,
\frac1{b_N(M_N-1)}
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}
(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)^\top .
\]
Let
\[
\widehat Y_j=Y_j-(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r)L_j,\qquad
\widehat B_{\ell,N}=\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}\widehat Y_j,
\]
and let \(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}\) be Algorithm~1's reported covariance obtained
from the \(\widehat B_{\ell,N}\)'s on \(\mathcal E_N\).  With the auxiliary
completion
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}
:=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N}\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
+\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{3\times3},
\]
one has
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=
O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
\]
In particular, for the paper's fixed protocol
\[
b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor,
\]
the plug-in covariance estimator itself satisfies, on the all-complete-cycle
reported event and with
\(\overline{\widehat B}_N=M_N^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}\widehat B_{\ell,N}\),
\[
\left\|
\frac{K_N}{N}\frac{1}{b_N(M_N-1)}
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)^\top
-\Sigma_r
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3}).
\]
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The displayed formula for \(U_j\) is the adjacent-gap form of the three
inclusion rewards: a half-open Palm cycle contains the click at its left
endpoint, a lag-one inclusion occurs exactly on \(G_j=0\), and a lag-two
inclusion occurs on \(G_j=1\) or on two consecutive zero gaps.  Palm inversion
for the local windows gives \(\EE U_j^{(m)}=r_m\EE L_j\), so \(\EE Y_j=0\).
Because \(Y_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\) and the gaps are i.i.d., \((Y_j)\) is
\(1\)-dependent.  The exponential gap moment from
\Cref{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main} gives uniformly finite
fourth moments for the normalized batch sums below.

First ignore plug-in centering and endpoint deletion.  For a deterministic
prefix \(n_N=\lfloor N/(\mu d_N)\rfloor\), put
\[
V_{\ell,N}=b_N^{-1/2}B_{\ell,N},\qquad 1\le\ell\le n_N .
\]
The omitted cycle \(\ell d_N-1\) separates the gap pairs entering adjacent
batch sums, hence the row variables \(V_{\ell,N}\) are independent.  Their
population covariance is
\[
\Gamma_N
=\frac1{b_N}\operatorname{Var}(B_{\ell,N})
=\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)
 +\left(1-\frac1{b_N}\right)
\{\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0)\},
\]
so
\[
\|\Gamma_N-T_1^{(r)}\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O(b_N^{-1}).
\]
Since \(\sup_{\ell,N}\EE\|V_{\ell,N}\|^4<\infty\), the ordinary sample second
moment and the empirical-mean subtraction satisfy
\[
\left\|
\frac1{n_N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_N}V_{\ell,N}V_{\ell,N}^\top-\Gamma_N
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}(n_N^{-1/2}),
\qquad
\|\bar V_N\bar V_N^\top\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O_{\PP}(n_N^{-1}),
\]
where \(\bar V_N=n_N^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{n_N}V_{\ell,N}\).  Replacing
\(n_N\) by \(n_N-1\) changes the covariance by \(O_{\PP}(n_N^{-1})\).  Thus the
deterministic-prefix, empirical-mean-subtracted batch covariance equals
\[
T_1^{(r)}+O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right),
\]
because \(n_N^{-1/2}=O(\sqrt{b_N/N})\).

Now restore the random calendar prefix.  The renewal CLT gives
\[
K_N=N/\mu+O_{\PP}(\sqrt N).
\]
The common all-complete-cycle set \(J_N\) removes only the initial incomplete
cycle, the terminal incomplete cycle, and the fixed lag-two look-ahead cycles;
their total calendar length is \(O_{\PP}(\log N)\), and they change the
retained separated-batch prefix only by endpoint batches plus the omitted
terminal partial batch.  Consequently
\[
M_Nd_N=K_N+O_{\PP}(d_N+\log N+1),
\qquad
\frac{M_N}{n_N}-1=O_{\PP}\left(\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
\]
On events where \(|M_N-n_N|\le Cn_N\sqrt{b_N/N}\), the added or removed
independent row variables lie in a deterministic window of this order.  Markov's
inequality, the uniform second-moment bound for \(V_{\ell,N}V_{\ell,N}^\top\),
and the same bound for \(V_{\ell,N}\) show that replacing the deterministic
prefix by \(M_N\) changes both the sample second moment and the empirical-mean
term by \(O_{\PP}(\sqrt{b_N/N})\).  The prefactor \(K_N/N\) then converts the
cycle-time covariance to calendar time:
\[
\frac{K_N}{N}=\frac1\mu+O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2}),
\]
and the \(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\) multiplication error is absorbed by
\(O_{\PP}(\sqrt{b_N/N})\).  Therefore
\[
\|\widetilde\Sigma_{r,N}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
\]

It remains to replace \(r\) by \(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\).  The
inclusion-coordinate CLT in
\Cref{lem:three-inclusion-clt-reduction-main} gives
\[
\Delta_N:=\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2}),
\qquad
\widehat Y_j-Y_j=-\Delta_N L_j .
\]
For polynomial \(b_N\), and hence for the batch lengths used in the
finite-record protocol, the uniform block-average estimate in
\Cref{lem:uniform-calendar-block-length-main} gives
\[
\max_{\ell\le M_N}\left\|\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}(\widehat Y_j-Y_j)\right\|
=O_{\PP}(b_NN^{-1/2}).
\]
Write this plug-in block error as \(P_{\ell,N}\), and subtract its empirical
mean \(\bar P_N\) in the same way as for \(B_{\ell,N}\).  The difference between
the plug-in covariance and the true-centered covariance is
\[
\frac{K_N}{N}\frac1{b_N(M_N-1)}
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}
\left\{(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)(P_{\ell,N}-\bar P_N)^\top
+(P_{\ell,N}-\bar P_N)(B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N)^\top
+(P_{\ell,N}-\bar P_N)(P_{\ell,N}-\bar P_N)^\top\right\}.
\]
The already proved true-centered covariance bound implies
\[
\frac1{b_NM_N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}\|B_{\ell,N}-\bar B_N\|^2=O_{\PP}(1),
\]
while the uniform plug-in block bound gives
\[
\frac1{b_NM_N}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}\|P_{\ell,N}-\bar P_N\|^2
=O_{\PP}(b_N/N).
\]
Cauchy's inequality bounds the two cross terms by
\(O_{\PP}(\sqrt{b_N/N})\), and the quadratic plug-in term by
\(O_{\PP}(b_N/N)\).  Since \(b_N/N\le\sqrt{b_N/N}\) eventually, the plug-in
perturbation is \(O_{\PP}(\sqrt{b_N/N})\).

Finally, \(\PP(\mathcal E_N^c)\to0\) at positive constrained D-MAP
population points by
\Cref{lem:finite-record-reporting-gate-main}.  Replacing the reported matrix on
\(\mathcal E_N^c\) by the auxiliary zero matrix therefore changes only a
vanishing-probability event in the \(O_{\PP}\) assertion.  Combining the
true-centered rate and the plug-in perturbation proves the claim.
\end{proof}

The next theorem records the D-MAP reward specialization and the retained-rank
consequence used by the main inferential theorems.

\begin{theorem}[D-MAP specialization of separated-batch covariance]\label{thm:finite-record-covariance-main}
Work under \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main} and the deterministic
threshold protocol \ref{fr:protocol-main} at a population point in the stable
quotient chart \(r_0>0\), \(D=r_1-r_0^2\ne0\).
For \(m=0,1,2\), let
\[
U_j^{(m)}=\sum_{n=\tau_j}^{\tau_{j+1}-1}A_nA_{n+m},
\qquad U_j^{(0)}=\sum_{n=\tau_j}^{\tau_{j+1}-1}A_n,
\]
on the all-complete-cycle set on which the displayed lagged terms are
observed.  Let
\[
r=(r_0,r_1,r_2)^\top,\qquad r_m=\PP(A_0=A_m=1),
\qquad L_j=\tau_{j+1}-\tau_j,
\]
and define the centered reward vector
\[
Y_j=
\bigl(U_j^{(0)}-r_0L_j,\,
      U_j^{(1)}-r_1L_j,\,
      U_j^{(2)}-r_2L_j\bigr)^\top .
\]
Equivalently, in Palm gap coordinates,
\[
U_j^{(0)}=1,\qquad
U_j^{(1)}=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},\qquad
U_j^{(2)}
=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,\ G_{j+1}=0\}}.
\]
Thus \(Y_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\), \((Y_j)\) is one-dependent,
\(\EE Y_j=0\), and
\[
\Sigma_r=\frac1\mu\left\{
\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)
+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0)\right\},
\qquad \mu=\EE L_0 ,
\]
is the calendar-time covariance in the inclusion-coordinate CLT.  Let the
separated batch length be deterministic,
\[
b_N=\lfloor N^\beta\rfloor,\qquad 0<\beta<1,
\]
and set \(d_N=b_N+1\).  The separated batch grid is
\[
I_{\ell,N}=\{(\ell-1)d_N,\ldots,(\ell-1)d_N+b_N-1\},
\qquad \ell\ge1,
\]
so the single complete-cycle reward with index \(\ell d_N-1\) is omitted as
the separator between adjacent batches.  Let
\[
\widehat r_N=(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}},
\widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}},
\widehat r_{2,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}})^\top .
\]
On the common all-complete-cycle set
\[
J_N=J_{0,N}\cap J_{1,N}\cap J_{2,N},
\]
use the observable plug-in centered reward vector
\[
\widehat Y_j=
\bigl(U_j^{(0)}-\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}L_j,\,
      U_j^{(1)}-\widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}L_j,\,
      U_j^{(2)}-\widehat r_{2,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}L_j\bigr)^\top .
\]
Let \(M_N\) be the retained all-complete-cycle batch count from Algorithm~1, so
unfinished terminal batches and endpoint/look-ahead cycles outside \(J_N\) are
omitted.  On the reporting event \(\mathcal E_N\), Algorithm~1 forms
\[
\widehat B_{\ell,N}=\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}\widehat Y_j,\qquad
\overline{\widehat B}_N=M_N^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}\widehat B_{\ell,N},
\]
and reports the single covariance estimator
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
=
\frac{K_N}{N}\,
\frac1{b_N(M_N-1)}
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)^\top .
\]
On \(\mathcal E_N^c\), no covariance matrix is reported.  For probability
limits use the auxiliary total-space completion
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}
:=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N}\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
+\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{3\times3}.
\]
Since \(\PP(\mathcal E_N^c)\to0\) at positive constrained D-MAP population
points by
\Cref{lem:finite-record-reporting-gate-main}, this completed matrix satisfies
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=
O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right)
=
O_{\PP}\left(N^{-\beta}+N^{-(1-\beta)/2}\right).
\]
With
\[
\omega_\beta:=\min\{\beta,(1-\beta)/2\},
\]
any rank-adaptive deterministic threshold \(t_N\) must satisfy
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=o_{\PP}(t_N).
\]
A concrete admissible choice is
\[
t_N=N^{-\alpha},
\qquad
0<\alpha<\omega_\beta;
\]
for the balanced choice \(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\), this becomes any
\(0<\alpha<1/3\), for instance \(t_N=N^{-1/4}\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The half-open Palm cycle \([\tau_j,\tau_{j+1})\) contains only the click at
\(\tau_j\), so \(U_j^{(0)}=1\).  A one-step look-ahead is a click exactly
when the next click occurs one sampled step later, so
\(U_j^{(1)}=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}}\).  For lag two, the contributing time is
again the click at \(\tau_j\); a click two sampled steps later occurs either
when \(G_j=1\), or when \(G_j=0\) and the next gap is also zero.  These
identities show that \(Y_j\) is a function of the adjacent gap pair
\((G_j,G_{j+1})\), hence is one-dependent.  Palm inversion in
\Cref{lem:local-window-regenerative-decomposition-main}, applied to
\(Z_n^{(m)}=A_nA_{n+m}\), gives
\(\EE U_j^{(m)}=r_m\EE L_j\), so \(\EE Y_j=0\).  The exponential moment from
\Cref{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main} gives the fourth
moments needed for the covariance proposition.  Applying
\Cref{prop:exact-inclusion-separated-batch-covariance-main} with this
adjacent-gap reward gives
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\left(b_N^{-1}+\sqrt{\frac{b_N}{N}}\right).
\]
That proposition is exactly the Algorithm~1 estimator: it uses the random
retained batch count \(M_N\), deletes the endpoint and fixed look-ahead cycles
through the common complete-cycle set \(J_N\), subtracts the empirical batch
mean, multiplies by \(K_N/N\), and controls the plug-in perturbation
\(\widehat Y_j-Y_j=-(\widehat r_N-r)L_j\).  Substituting
\(b_N=\lfloor N^\beta\rfloor\), \(0<\beta<1\), gives
\[
O_{\PP}\left(N^{-\beta}+N^{-(1-\beta)/2}\right).
\]
The threshold claim is immediate from Weyl's eigenvalue inequality: every
positive limiting eigenvalue stays above \(t_N\), and every zero limiting
eigenvalue falls below \(t_N\), with probability tending to one whenever
\(\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=o_{\PP}(t_N)\).
\end{proof}


\begin{lemma}[Conditional projected-Gaussian law at a moving half-space]\label{lem:projected-gaussian-bootstrap-halfspace-main}
For \(t\in(0,1)\), write
\[
H_t=\{h\in\RR^2:h_2\le th_1\}.
\]
Let \(z_N\to z\in(0,1)\) in probability and let
\(\widehat\Sigma_N\) be a symmetric nonnegative definite covariance estimator
such that
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_N-\Sigma\|_{\operatorname{op}}=o_{\PP}(t_N),
\qquad t_N\downarrow0,
\]
for a fixed nonnegative definite \(2\times2\) matrix \(\Sigma\).  Let
\[
\widehat\Sigma_N^+
=\sum_{\widehat\lambda_{i,N}>t_N}
\widehat\lambda_{i,N}^{-1}
\widehat v_{i,N}\widehat v_{i,N}^{\top}
\]
be the thresholded Moore--Penrose inverse.  Suppose that
\[
X_N\Rightarrow X:=\Pi_{H_z}(Z),
\qquad Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma).
\]
This is the local constrained-estimation limit: the Gaussian perturbation is
projected onto the tangent half-space before the quadratic statistic is formed.
It is the same projected-normal structure that appears in constrained
\(M\)-estimation limits \cite{Geyer1994ConstrainedM}.  The simulation below is
therefore not an ordinary nonparametric or parametric bootstrap from the
record; it is the conditional plug-in projected Gaussian law, with retained
empirical rank.
Define
\[
T_N=X_N^\top\widehat\Sigma_N^+X_N
\]
and, conditionally on the observed record,
\[
Z_N^\ast\sim \mathcal N(0,\widehat\Sigma_N),\qquad
X_N^\ast=\Pi_{H_{z_N}}(Z_N^\ast),\qquad
T_N^\ast=(X_N^\ast)^\top\widehat\Sigma_N^+X_N^\ast .
\]
Then
\[
T_N\Rightarrow T:=X^\top\Sigma^+X,
\]
and the conditional law of \(T_N^\ast\) converges weakly in probability to
the law of \(T\).  Consequently, if \(c_{1-\alpha,N}^{\ast,-}\) is the lower
conditional \((1-\alpha)\)-quantile of \(T_N^\ast\) and the limiting quantile
\[
c_{1-\alpha}:=\inf\{c:\PP(T\le c)\ge1-\alpha\}
\]
is a continuity point of the distribution function of \(T\), then
\[
c_{1-\alpha,N}^{\ast,-}\to c_{1-\alpha}
\quad\text{in probability}.
\]
If in addition \(\PP(T\le c_{1-\alpha})=1-\alpha\), then
\[
\PP\{T_N\le c_{1-\alpha,N}^{\ast,-}\}\to1-\alpha .
\]
If \(\Sigma=0\), then the retained-rank inverse is the zero matrix with
probability tending to one; consequently \(T_N=0\) and \(T_N^\ast=0\) with
probability tending to one, and the fixed positive-buffer lower-coverage
conclusion follows from
\Cref{lem:buffered-random-quantile-coverage-main}.

\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Weyl's inequality and the condition
\(\|\widehat\Sigma_N-\Sigma\|_{\operatorname{op}}=o_{\PP}(t_N)\) imply that,
with probability tending to one, every positive eigenvalue of \(\Sigma\) is
retained and every zero eigenvalue is removed by the threshold.  On that event
the retained spectral projection converges to the range projection of
\(\Sigma\), and
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_N^+-\Sigma^+\|_{\operatorname{op}}\to0
\]
in probability.  Slutsky's theorem therefore gives
\[
X_N^\top\widehat\Sigma_N^+X_N
\Rightarrow X^\top\Sigma^+X .
\]
In the special case \(\Sigma=0\), the same eigenvalue bound says
\[
\PP\{\|\widehat\Sigma_N\|_{\operatorname{op}}\le t_N\}\to1,
\]
so the thresholded retained-rank inverse is exactly zero with probability
tending to one.  Hence \(T_N=0\) and, conditionally, \(T_N^\ast=0\) on that
high-probability event.  The limiting distribution is the point mass at zero,
whose lower \(p\)-quantile is zero for every \(0<p<1\); the positive-buffer
coverage statement is therefore the buffered random-quantile conclusion.

The remaining issue is conditional Gaussian convergence after the moving
projection.  The half-space projection has the explicit formula
\[
\Pi_{H_t}(u)
=u-\frac{(u_2-tu_1)_+}{1+t^2}\binom{-t}{1}.
\]
Thus \(u\mapsto\Pi_{H_t}(u)\) is one-Lipschitz and
\((t,u)\mapsto\Pi_{H_t}(u)\) is continuous, uniformly for \(t\) in compact
subintervals of \((0,1)\) and \(u\) in bounded sets.  Since
\(\widehat\Sigma_N\to\Sigma\) in probability, the conditional Gaussian laws
\(\mathcal N(0,\widehat\Sigma_N)\) converge weakly in probability to
\(\mathcal N(0,\Sigma)\).  Combining this Gaussian continuity with
\(z_N\to z\) and \(\widehat\Sigma_N^+\to\Sigma^+\) shows that, for every
bounded continuous \(\varphi:\RR\to\RR\),
\[
\EE^\ast\varphi(T_N^\ast)
\to
\EE\varphi\!\left((\Pi_{H_z}Z)^\top\Sigma^+\Pi_{H_z}Z\right)
\]
in probability, where \(\EE^\ast\) denotes conditional expectation given the
record.  This is conditional weak convergence in probability.  The quantile
conclusion is the standard consequence of weak convergence at a continuity
point of the limiting distribution function; the final coverage statement
then follows from the convergence of \(T_N\) and the random quantile
\(c_{1-\alpha,N}^{\ast,-}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Buffered random-quantile lower coverage]\label{lem:buffered-random-quantile-coverage-main}
Let \(T_N\Rightarrow T\), and let \(F_N^\ast\) be random conditional
distribution functions such that
\[
F_N^\ast(t)\to F(t):=\PP(T\le t)
\qquad\text{in probability}
\]
at every continuity point \(t\) of \(F\).  Fix \(0<p<1\), and define the
lower random conditional \(p\)-quantile and the limiting lower \(p\)-quantile
by
\[
c_N^-=\inf\{t:F_N^\ast(t)\ge p\},
\qquad
c^-=\inf\{t:F(t)\ge p\}.
\]
Assume \(c^-\) is finite.  Then, for every fixed \(\varepsilon>0\),
\[
\liminf_{N\to\infty}\PP\{T_N\le c_N^-+\varepsilon\}\ge p .
\]
No continuity of \(F\) at \(c^-\), and no equality \(F(c^-)=p\), is required.
In particular, this lemma applies to the diagonal projected-Gaussian
zero-quantile alternatives after replacing \(p\) by \(1-\alpha\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
First the random lower quantile cannot fall a fixed distance below the
limiting lower quantile.  Fix \(\delta>0\).  By the definition of \(c^-\),
\(F(c^--\delta)<p\).  Choose a continuity point
\[
x\in(c^--\delta,c^-)
\]
with \(F(x)<p\); distribution functions have at most countably many
discontinuities, so such a point is available after decreasing \(x\) slightly
if needed.  The pointwise conditional convergence gives
\[
F_N^\ast(x)\to F(x)
\qquad\text{in probability}.
\]
For any distribution function \(G\), the event
\(\inf\{t:G(t)\ge p\}\le x\) implies \(G(x)\ge p\).  Hence
\[
\PP\{c_N^-\le x\}
\le
\PP\{F_N^\ast(x)\ge p\}\to0,
\]
and \(c_N^->x\) with probability tending to one.

Choose \(x>c^- -\varepsilon\), and then choose a continuity point
\[
y\in(c^-,x+\varepsilon).
\]
This is possible because \(x+\varepsilon>c^-\).  On the event
\(\{c_N^->x\}\), the inclusion
\[
\{T_N\le y\}\subseteq\{T_N\le c_N^-+\varepsilon\}
\]
holds.  Therefore
\[
\liminf_N\PP\{T_N\le c_N^-+\varepsilon\}
\ge
\liminf_N\PP\{T_N\le y\}.
\]
Weak convergence at the continuity point \(y\) gives
\[
\lim_N\PP\{T_N\le y\}=F(y).
\]
Finally \(y>c^-\) implies \(F(y)\ge p\) by the lower-quantile definition.
The displayed lower bound follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Conditional Monte Carlo diagnostic and coverage transfer]\label{lem:conditional-monte-carlo-quantile-main}
Fix \(0<z<1\) and condition on the observed record.  Let
\[
T_N^\ast(z)=
\Pi_{H_z}(Z_N^\ast)^\top\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{+}
\Pi_{H_z}(Z_N^\ast),
\qquad
Z_N^\ast\mid A_0,\ldots,A_{N-1}\sim
\mathcal N(0,\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}),
\]
where \(H_z=\{h:h_2\le zh_1\}\), and let
\[
F_N^\ast(t;z)=\PP^\ast\{T_N^\ast(z)\le t\}
\]
be its conditional distribution function.  If
\(T_{N,1}^\ast(z),\ldots,T_{N,R}^\ast(z)\) are conditionally independent draws
from this law and
\[
\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast(t;z)
=\frac1R\sum_{m=1}^R\mathbf1_{\{T_{N,m}^\ast(z)\le t\}},
\]
then for every \(\varepsilon>0\),
\[
\PP^\ast\!\left(
\sup_{t\in\mathbb R}
\left|\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast(t;z)-F_N^\ast(t;z)\right|>\varepsilon
\right)
\le 2e^{-2R\varepsilon^2}.
\]
Equivalently, for every \(0<\delta<1\), with conditional probability at least
\(1-\delta\),
\[
\sup_t|\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast(t;z)-F_N^\ast(t;z)|
\le
\varepsilon_{R,\delta}:=
\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2R}} .
\]
Let \(p_\alpha=1-\alpha\), let
\[
c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)
=\inf\{t:F_N^\ast(t;z)\ge p_\alpha\},
\qquad
\widehat c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z)
=T_{N,(\lceil p_\alpha R\rceil)}^\ast(z),
\]
where the draws are sorted increasingly.  If \(c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)\) is a
continuity point of \(F_N^\ast(\cdot;z)\), then
\[
\widehat c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z)
\longrightarrow c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)
\qquad\text{in conditional probability as }R\to\infty .
\]
If the conditional law has an atom or a flat stretch at the lower quantile,
the raw order statistic still estimates a lower conditional quantile in the
usual empirical-quantile sense, but the raw order statistic at level
\(p_\alpha\) is not by itself a one-sided coverage certificate.  A DKW
certificate is obtained by spending probability in the distribution-function
scale.  If
\[
\varepsilon_{R,\delta}<\alpha,\qquad
p_{\alpha,\delta,R}^{+}:=p_\alpha+\varepsilon_{R,\delta}<1,
\]
put \(q_R=\widehat c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z)\) and define
\[
d^{\mathrm{DKW}}_{R,\delta,N}(z)
:=
\inf\left\{d\ge0:
\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast(q_R+d;z)-\varepsilon_{R,\delta}
\ge 1-\alpha\right\}.
\]
Equivalently, since the empirical distribution is a step function, the
inflated lower empirical quantile is
\[
\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R,\delta}(z)
:=T_{N,(\lceil p_{\alpha,\delta,R}^{+}R\rceil)}^\ast(z),
\]
and \(q_R+d^{\mathrm{DKW}}_{R,\delta,N}(z)
=\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R,\delta}(z)\).
Then, conditionally on the observed record,
\[
\PP^\ast\!\left\{
q_R+d^{\mathrm{DKW}}_{R,\delta,N}(z)
\ge c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)\right\}\ge1-\delta .
\]
Equivalently, the inflated simulated critical value is the empirical lower
\((p_\alpha+\varepsilon_{R,\delta})\)-quantile.  This conclusion is
distribution-free and does not require continuity or a positive density at the
ideal conditional quantile.

For the coverage-transfer statement, fix the true diagonal coordinate \(z_0\).
Let \(G_N=G_{\mathrm{cert},N}(z_0)\) be the record-level reporting event on
which the population branch, ideal-quantile enclosure, and numerical recipe
are certified.  A finite report may set \(G_N=1\) only when the manuscript or
attached transcript contains the branch certificate and the ideal conditional
quantile enclosure used by the reported inequality; otherwise the event is
declared false for coverage-certification purposes.  Let
\[
H_N=G_{\mathrm{MC},N}(z_0)
=\{\widehat c^-_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z_0)
+d_{R,\delta_N,N}(z_0)\ge c^-_{1-\alpha,N}(z_0)\}
\]
be the auxiliary one-sided Monte Carlo domination event.  The event \(H_N\)
is part of the report only through a displayed one-sided certificate, for
example the DKW event and inflation in the preceding display.  Suppose
\[
\PP(G_N)\to1,\qquad \delta_N\to0,
\]
and on \(G_N\),
\[
\PP^\ast\{\widehat c^-_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z_0)
+d_{R,\delta_N,N}(z_0)\ge c^-_{1-\alpha,N}(z_0)\}\ge1-\delta_N .
\]
Define the fixed-candidate ideal and simulated diagonal acceptance events by
\[
\mathcal A_N(z_0)
:=\{Q_N(z_0)\le c^-_{1-\alpha,N}(z_0)\},
\]
and
\[
\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{MC}}(z_0)
:=\{Q_N(z_0)\le
\widehat c^-_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z_0)+d_{R,\delta_N,N}(z_0)\}.
\]
Then
\[
\PP\{\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{MC}}(z_0)\}
\ge
\PP\{\mathcal A_N(z_0)\}
-\delta_N-\PP(G_N^c)-o(1).
\]
Therefore, if the ideal event has limiting coverage \(1-\alpha\), the simulated
event has
\[
\liminf_N
\PP\{\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{MC}}(z_0)\}
\ge1-\alpha .
\]
Without such an explicit event \(G_{\mathrm{MC},N}(z_0)\) and one-sided
inflation \(d_{R,\delta_N,N}(z_0)\), finite simulation remains only a numerical
diagnostic for the ideal conditional projected-Gaussian law.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Conditional on the record, the variables
\(T_{N,1}^\ast(z),\ldots,T_{N,R}^\ast(z)\) are independent real-valued draws
with distribution function \(F_N^\ast(\cdot;z)\).  The displayed exponential
bound is therefore the Dvoretzky--Kiefer--Wolfowitz inequality, in Massart's
sharp-constant form, applied under \(\PP^\ast\) \cite{Massart1990DKW}.

Let
\[
\Delta_{N,R}(z)=
\sup_t|\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast(t;z)-F_N^\ast(t;z)|.
\]
The DKW bound gives \(\Delta_{N,R}(z)=O_{\PP^\ast}(R^{-1/2})\).  At a
continuity point of the conditional quantile, the Glivenko--Cantelli
consequence of this bound gives the standard empirical-quantile consistency:
for every \(\eta>0\), after decreasing \(\eta\) if necessary on the left,
\[
F_N^\ast(c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)-\eta;z)<p_\alpha
\le F_N^\ast(c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)+\eta;z).
\]
Uniform convergence of \(\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast\) to \(F_N^\ast\) then forces
the order statistic into this \(\eta\)-neighbourhood with conditional
probability tending to one.  At a discontinuity or flat quantile the same DKW
bound controls the simulated distribution function but the uninflated
\(p_\alpha\)-order statistic need not dominate the ideal lower quantile.

The DKW-inflated statistic gives a one-sided certificate without any local
regularity.  On the event
\[
B_{R,\delta,N}(z)=
\left\{\Delta_{N,R}(z)\le\varepsilon_{R,\delta}\right\},
\]
we have, with
\[
\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R,\delta}(z)
=T_{N,(\lceil(p_\alpha+\varepsilon_{R,\delta})R\rceil)}^\ast(z),
\]
that
\[
\widehat F_{N,R}^\ast
\bigl(\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R,\delta}(z);z\bigr)
\ge p_\alpha+\varepsilon_{R,\delta}.
\]
Therefore
\[
F_N^\ast
\bigl(\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R,\delta}(z);z\bigr)
\ge p_\alpha .
\]
By the definition of the lower quantile,
\[
c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)
\le
\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R,\delta}(z)
=q_R
+d^{\mathrm{DKW}}_{R,\delta,N}(z).
\]
Since the DKW inequality gives \(\PP^\ast(B_{R,\delta,N}(z))\ge1-\delta\),
the stated DKW domination bound follows.  The only deterministic size
condition needed for this construction is
\(\varepsilon_{R,\delta}<\alpha\), which ensures that the empirical
\((p_\alpha+\varepsilon_{R,\delta})\)-quantile is below level one.

It remains to prove the coverage-transfer inequality.
On \(G_N\cap H_N\), the ideal fixed-candidate event is contained in the
simulated event, because the simulated critical value is at least as large as
the ideal one.  Taking conditional expectation over the auxiliary Monte Carlo
draws gives
\[
\PP\{\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{MC}}(z_0)\}
\ge
\PP\{\mathcal A_N(z_0),G_N\}
-\EE\{1_{G_N}\PP^\ast(H_N^c)\}.
\]
The assumed conditional bound on \(G_N\) makes the last term at most
\(\delta_N\).  Since
\[
\PP\{\mathcal A_N(z_0),G_N\}
\ge
\PP\{\mathcal A_N(z_0)\}-\PP(G_N^c),
\]
the displayed inequality follows, in fact without the harmless \(o(1)\) term
except for any asymptotically negligible convention used to make the finite
Monte Carlo rule measurable on nonreporting records.  The final \(\liminf\)
claim follows from \(\PP(G_N^c)\to0\), \(\delta_N\to0\), and the assumed ideal
coverage limit.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Deterministic full-rank projected-Gaussian quantile]\label{lem:deterministic-full-rank-projected-gaussian-quantile-main}
Fix \(0<z<1\), let
\[
H_z=\{u\in\mathbb R^2:u_2\le zu_1\},\qquad
n_z=(-z,1)^\top,
\]
and let \(Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma)\), where \(\Sigma\) is a positive
definite \(2\times2\) matrix.  For \(c\in\mathbb R\), define
\[
F_{z,\Sigma}(c)=
\PP\{Z\in H_z,\ Z^\top\Sigma^{-1}Z\le c\}
+\PP\{Z\notin H_z,\ 
(\Pi_{H_z}Z)^\top\Sigma^{-1}(\Pi_{H_z}Z)\le c\}.
\]
Equivalently, with Gaussian density \(\varphi_\Sigma\) and with
\[
\Pi_{H_z}u=
\begin{cases}
u,&n_z^\top u\le0,\\[0.2em]
u-\dfrac{n_z^\top u}{\|n_z\|^2}n_z,&n_z^\top u>0,
\end{cases}
\]
the same function is the deterministic integral
\[
F_{z,\Sigma}(c)
=\int_{H_z}
\mathbf1_{\{u^\top\Sigma^{-1}u\le c\}}\varphi_\Sigma(u)\,du
+\int_{H_z^c}
\mathbf1_{\{(\Pi_{H_z}u)^\top\Sigma^{-1}(\Pi_{H_z}u)\le c\}}
\varphi_\Sigma(u)\,du .
\]
It is the distribution function of
\[
T_{z,\Sigma}=(\Pi_{H_z}Z)^\top\Sigma^{-1}(\Pi_{H_z}Z).
\]
Hence \(F_{z,\Sigma}(c)=0\) for \(c<0\), \(F_{z,\Sigma}\) is nondecreasing
and right-continuous, and \(\lim_{c\to\infty}F_{z,\Sigma}(c)=1\).  For
\(0<p<1\), the deterministic lower projected-Gaussian quantile is
\[
c_p(z,\Sigma):=\inf\{c\ge0:F_{z,\Sigma}(c)\ge p\}.
\]
This number is finite and satisfies
\[
\PP\{T_{z,\Sigma}\le c_p(z,\Sigma)\}\ge p,\qquad
\PP\{T_{z,\Sigma}<c_p(z,\Sigma)\}\le p.
\]
Moreover \(F_{z,\Sigma}\) has no atoms; in particular
\(\PP\{T_{z,\Sigma}=c_p(z,\Sigma)\}=0\) and
\(\PP\{T_{z,\Sigma}\le c_p(z,\Sigma)\}=p\).  It is also strictly
increasing on \([0,\infty)\): if \(0\le a<b\), then
\(F_{z,\Sigma}(b)>F_{z,\Sigma}(a)\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The displayed projection formula is the Euclidean projection onto the closed
half-space \(H_z\), so the two integrals partition the sample space into the
inside and outside events and give exactly the distribution function of
\(T_{z,\Sigma}\).  Distribution functions are nondecreasing and
right-continuous.  Since the quadratic form is nonnegative and finite almost
surely, \(F_{z,\Sigma}(c)=0\) for \(c<0\) and
\(\lim_{c\to\infty}F_{z,\Sigma}(c)=1\), which makes the displayed lower
quantile finite for every \(0<p<1\).  The two lower-quantile inequalities are
the standard consequences of the definition of
\(\inf\{c:F_{z,\Sigma}(c)\ge p\}\) and right-continuity.

It remains only to record why no atom is hidden in the projection.  On \(H_z\),
the statistic is the nonzero quadratic polynomial \(u^\top\Sigma^{-1}u\).  On
\(H_z^c\), the projection is the linear map
\[
L_z u=u-\frac{n_z^\top u}{\|n_z\|^2}n_z
\]
and the statistic is the nonzero quadratic polynomial
\(u^\top L_z^\top\Sigma^{-1}L_z u\).  For each fixed \(c\), the corresponding
level set in either half-plane has two-dimensional Lebesgue measure zero:
for \(c>0\) it is contained in a conic section, and for \(c=0\) it is a line
or the origin.  The full-rank Gaussian law has a Lebesgue density, so
\(\PP\{T_{z,\Sigma}=c\}=0\) for every \(c\).  Therefore the lower quantile is
an exact \(p\)-quantile.  To see strict increase, fix \(0\le a<b\).  The
half-space \(H_z\) has nonempty interior, and the open set
\[
\{u\in\operatorname{int}H_z:a<u^\top\Sigma^{-1}u<b\}
\]
is nonempty.  On this set \(T_{z,\Sigma}=u^\top\Sigma^{-1}u\), and the
full-rank Gaussian density is strictly positive.  Hence
\(\PP\{a<T_{z,\Sigma}<b\}>0\), which proves the claimed strict increase.
\end{proof}

\begin{algorithmiclemma}[Certified deterministic projected-Gaussian quantile]\label{lem:certified-deterministic-projected-gaussian-quantile-main}
Fix \(0<z<1\), \(0<p<1\), and a positive definite \(2\times2\) covariance
matrix \(\Sigma\).  Suppose the data used by the numerical report are given by
outward-rounded interval enclosures \([z]\), \([\Sigma]\), and \([Q]\), where
\([Q]\) contains the computed statistic \(Q\).  Then the lower quantile
\[
c_p(z,\Sigma)=\inf\{c\ge0:F_{z,\Sigma}(c)\ge p\}
\]
from \Cref{lem:deterministic-full-rank-projected-gaussian-quantile-main} has
a reproducible deterministic certificate of the following form.  For any
requested probability error \(\eta_F>0\) and abscissa tolerance \(\eta_c>0\),
there is a finite outward-rounded computation which returns numbers
\[
L<U,\qquad U-L\le\eta_c,
\]
such that
\[
F_{z,\Sigma}(L)<p\le F_{z,\Sigma}(U),
\qquad\text{and hence}\qquad
c_p(z,\Sigma)\in(L,U].
\]
Moreover the same certificate gives a rigorous comparison of \(Q\) with the
ideal quantile whenever the certified intervals are separated:
\[
[Q]^+\le L\quad\Longrightarrow\quad Q<c_p(z,\Sigma),
\]
and
\[
[Q]^->U\quad\Longrightarrow\quad Q>c_p(z,\Sigma).
\]
If neither separation holds, the routine returns the certified statement
\[
Q-c_p(z,\Sigma)\in
\bigl[[Q]^--U,\,[Q]^+-L\bigr],
\]
so the only undecided cases are those lying within the declared numerical
quantile/statistic enclosure.

The computation is the following one.  First compute an outward-rounded
Cholesky enclosure \([C]\) for \(\Sigma=C C^\top\), together with interval
enclosures for \(\Sigma^{-1}\).  Write \(Y\sim\mathcal N(0,I_2)\) and
\(Z=CY\).  Choose \(R>0\) so that the certified Mills-ratio bound
\begin{equation}\label{eq:certified-gaussian-tail-truncation-main}
4\,\frac{\exp(-R^2/2)}{R\sqrt{2\pi}}\le\eta_{\rm tail}
\end{equation}
holds, with \(0<\eta_{\rm tail}<\eta_F/4\).  Then
\[
\PP\{\|Y\|_\infty>R\}\le\eta_{\rm tail},
\]
so all probability outside the square \(S_R=[-R,R]^2\) is charged only to the
upper distribution-function bound.

For a candidate value \(c\ge0\), partition \(S_R\) into finitely many closed
axis-parallel boxes \(B\).  On each box use directed interval arithmetic to
enclose
\[
s_B=n_{[z]}^\top [C]B,\qquad
q_B^{\rm in}=([C]B)^\top[\Sigma^{-1}]([C]B),
\]
and, for the exterior projected region,
\[
[P_z u]
=u-\frac{(u_2-[z]u_1)_+}{1+[z]^2}\binom{-[z]}{1},
\qquad
q_B^{\rm out}=([P_z C B])^\top[\Sigma^{-1}]([P_z C B]).
\]
The lower sum \(F^-(c)\) adds the exact product-normal mass
\[
\gamma(B)=
\{\Phi(b_1)-\Phi(a_1)\}\{\Phi(b_2)-\Phi(a_2)\},
\qquad B=[a_1,b_1]\times[a_2,b_2],
\]
for every box certified to lie in the first projected region and satisfy
\((q_B^{\rm in})^+\le c\), and for every box certified to lie in the second
projected region and satisfy \((q_B^{\rm out})^+\le c\).  The upper sum
\(F^+(c)\) adds \(\gamma(B)\) for every box not certified to be outside the
corresponding event, namely for boxes whose interval tests do not prove
\(s_B>0\) or \((q_B^{\rm in})^->c\) in the inside integral, and do not prove
\(s_B\le0\) or \((q_B^{\rm out})^->c\) in the outside integral.  Finally set
\[
F^-(c)=\sum_{\text{certified inside/outside accepted boxes}}\gamma(B),
\qquad
F^+(c)=\eta_{\rm tail}+\sum_{\text{not certified rejected boxes}}\gamma(B).
\]
Then
\[
F^-(c)\le F_{z,\Sigma}(c)\le F^+(c).
\]
The partition is refined until \(F^+(c)-F^-(c)\le\eta_F\).

With this certified distribution-function oracle, choose an initial bracket
\([a,b]\) by setting \(a=0\) and increasing \(b\) until
\[
F^-(b)\ge p .
\]
If the current lower endpoint does not satisfy \(F^+(a)<p\), bisect
\([0,b]\) until an endpoint with \(F^+(\cdot)<p\) is found; such an endpoint
exists because \(F_{z,\Sigma}(0)=0<p\).  Thereafter bisect
\([a,b]\).  For a midpoint \(m\), replace \(a\) by \(m\) when
\[
F^+(m)<p,
\]
and replace \(b\) by \(m\) when
\[
F^-(m)\ge p.
\]
If neither inequality is certified, refine the quadrature tolerance at this
same \(m\) and repeat.  If \(m\) remains unresolved because it lies at the
target distribution level, replace \(m\) by two adjacent rational points
\(m_-<m<m_+\) inside the current bracket, with \(m_+-m_-<\eta_c/2\), and
resolve those two points instead; the continuity proved in
\Cref{lem:deterministic-full-rank-projected-gaussian-quantile-main} guarantees
that arbitrarily close one-sided resolved points exist.  The bisection stops
when \(b-a\le\eta_c\), and returns \((L,U)=(a,b)\).
\end{algorithmiclemma}

\begin{proof}
The transformation \(Z=CY\) reduces the Gaussian density to the product
standard-normal density.  The bound
\eqref{eq:certified-gaussian-tail-truncation-main} and the union bound give
the displayed square-truncation error.  On a retained box \(B\), directed
interval arithmetic encloses every value of the half-space sign, the
unprojected quadratic form on \(H_z\), and the projected quadratic form on
\(H_z^c\).  A box added to the lower sum is therefore wholly contained in one
of the two defining events for \(F_{z,\Sigma}(c)\), and a box removed from the
upper sum is wholly contained in the complement of the relevant event.  The
remaining boxes are charged to the upper sum, so
\[
F^-(c)\le F_{z,\Sigma}(c)\le F^+(c)
\]
after adding the tail only to the upper side.

It remains to justify finite termination of each distribution-function
enclosure.  For fixed \(c\), the ambiguous set
is contained, up to an arbitrarily small rectangular neighbourhood, in the
union of the line \(n_z^\top C y=0\) and the two quadratic level curves
\((Cy)^\top\Sigma^{-1}(Cy)=c\) and
\((\Pi_{H_z}Cy)^\top\Sigma^{-1}(\Pi_{H_z}Cy)=c\).  These sets have
two-dimensional Lebesgue measure zero.  Since the standard-normal density is
bounded on \(S_R\) and the Gaussian measure is regular, sufficiently fine
rectangular partitions make the total Gaussian mass of undecided boxes below
any prescribed positive value.  Hence the refinement loop achieves
\(F^+(c)-F^-(c)\le\eta_F\) in finitely many steps.

The initial upper endpoint exists because
\(F_{z,\Sigma}(c)\to1\) as \(c\to\infty\), and \(p<1\).  The certified
inequalities \(F^+(a)<p\) and \(F^-(b)\ge p\) imply
\[
F_{z,\Sigma}(a)<p,\qquad F_{z,\Sigma}(b)\ge p,
\]
and therefore \(a<c_p(z,\Sigma)\le b\).  Each bisection step preserves this
invariant.  If a midpoint is unresolved, decreasing \(\eta_F\) and refining
the quadrature eventually resolves it unless
\(F_{z,\Sigma}(m)=p\); in that exceptional case the algorithm uses
neighbouring rational split points \(m_-<m<m_+\).  Either side of \(m\) can be
bracketed by arbitrarily close resolved points because
\Cref{lem:deterministic-full-rank-projected-gaussian-quantile-main} proves
that the law has no atoms and is strictly increasing.  Thus the interval can be shortened to any
prescribed \(\eta_c\).  The final comparison with \([Q]\) follows immediately
from \(L<c_p(z,\Sigma)\le U\) and the fact that \(Q\in[Q]\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Certified projected-Gaussian quantile algorithm]\label{thm:certified-projected-gaussian-quantile-algorithm-main}
Fix \(0<z<1\), a positive definite \(2\times2\) covariance matrix
\(\Sigma\), and \(0<p<1\).  Put
\[
H_z=\{u\in\RR^2:u_2\le zu_1\},\qquad
\Pi_{H_z}u=u-\frac{(u_2-zu_1)_+}{1+z^2}(-z,1)^\top ,
\]
and, with \(\phi_\Sigma\) denoting the density of
\(\mathcal N(0,\Sigma)\), define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:certified-projected-gaussian-cdf-main}
F_{z,\Sigma}(c)
:=\int_{H_z}\mathbf1\{u^\top\Sigma^{-1}u\le c\}\phi_\Sigma(u)\,du
+\int_{H_z^c}
\mathbf1\{(\Pi_{H_z}u)^\top\Sigma^{-1}(\Pi_{H_z}u)\le c\}
\phi_\Sigma(u)\,du .
\end{equation}
The lower projected-Gaussian quantile
\[
c_p(z,\Sigma):=\inf\{c\ge0:F_{z,\Sigma}(c)\ge p\}
\]
is a reproducible mathematical object in the following finite-certificate
sense.

For every pair of tolerances \(\eta_F,\eta_c>0\), the deterministic procedure
of
\Cref{lem:certified-deterministic-projected-gaussian-quantile-main} produces
a finite transcript consisting of outward-rounded enclosures for \(z\),
\(\Sigma\), \(\Sigma^{-1}\), a Cholesky factor \(C\), a certified tail square
\([-R,R]^2\), a finite box partition of that square, interval lower and upper
distribution sums \(F^-(c)\le F_{z,\Sigma}(c)\le F^+(c)\), and bisection
endpoints \(L<U\) satisfying
\[
U-L\le\eta_c,\qquad F^+(L)<p,\qquad F^-(U)\ge p .
\]
Consequently
\[
L<c_p(z,\Sigma)\le U .
\]
If a computed statistic \(Q\) is given by an outward-rounded interval
\([Q]\), the same transcript certifies the comparison with the ideal quantile:
\[
[Q]^+\le L\Rightarrow Q<c_p(z,\Sigma),\qquad
[Q]^->U\Rightarrow Q>c_p(z,\Sigma),
\]
and otherwise certifies only the enclosure
\[
Q-c_p(z,\Sigma)\in[[Q]^--U,[Q]^+-L].
\]

Moreover, conditional finite Monte Carlo simulation is coverage-certified only
after a one-sided domination certificate is attached.  Given conditionally
independent draws \(T_1^\ast,\ldots,T_R^\ast\) from the law with distribution
function \(F_{z,\Sigma}\), let
\[
\widehat F_R(t)=R^{-1}\sum_{m=1}^R\mathbf1_{\{T_m^\ast\le t\}},\qquad
q_R=T_{(\lceil pR\rceil)}^\ast ,
\]
with the draws sorted increasingly, and let
\[
\varepsilon_{R,\delta}:=\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta)}{2R}},\qquad
d_{R,\delta}:=
\inf\{d\ge0:\widehat F_R(q_R+d)-\varepsilon_{R,\delta}\ge p\}.
\]
If \(p+\varepsilon_{R,\delta}<1\), then
\[
\PP^\ast\{q_R+d_{R,\delta}\ge c_p(z,\Sigma)\}\ge1-\delta .
\]
Thus a raw simulated quantile is diagnostic by itself; the reproducible
coverage-certified simulated critical value is the inflated value
\(q_R+d_{R,\delta}\), or another value accompanied by an explicitly proved
one-sided domination event of the same form.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
The identity \eqref{eq:certified-projected-gaussian-cdf-main} is the
distribution function of
\((\Pi_{H_z}Z)^\top\Sigma^{-1}\Pi_{H_z}Z\),
\(Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma)\), by
\Cref{lem:deterministic-full-rank-projected-gaussian-quantile-main}.  The
same lemma proves that \(F_{z,\Sigma}\) is continuous and strictly increasing
on \([0,\infty)\).  The interval-integration and bisection procedure in
\Cref{lem:certified-deterministic-projected-gaussian-quantile-main} constructs
the displayed \(F^-,F^+\) and endpoint inequalities.  These inequalities imply
\[
F_{z,\Sigma}(L)\le F^+(L)<p,\qquad
F_{z,\Sigma}(U)\ge F^-(U)\ge p,
\]
so the lower quantile lies in \((L,U]\).  The three comparison statements with
\([Q]\) are then immediate from \(Q\in[Q]\) and \(L<c_p(z,\Sigma)\le U\).

For the simulation statement, the Dvoretzky--Kiefer--Wolfowitz inequality gives
\[
\PP^\ast\{\sup_t|\widehat F_R(t)-F_{z,\Sigma}(t)|
\le\varepsilon_{R,\delta}\}\ge1-\delta .
\]
On this event the defining inequality for \(d_{R,\delta}\) gives
\[
F_{z,\Sigma}(q_R+d_{R,\delta})
\ge \widehat F_R(q_R+d_{R,\delta})-\varepsilon_{R,\delta}
\ge p .
\]
By the definition of the lower quantile,
\(q_R+d_{R,\delta}\ge c_p(z,\Sigma)\).  The stated conditional probability
bound follows.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Projected-Gaussian continuity and rank degeneracy]\label{thm:projected-gaussian-continuity-degeneracy-main}
Fix \(0<z<1\), put
\[
n_z=(-z,1)^\top,\qquad H_z=\{h:n_z^\top h\le0\},
\]
and let
\[
T_z=(\Pi_{H_z}Z)^\top\Sigma^+\Pi_{H_z}Z,
\qquad Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma),
\]
where \(\Sigma\) is a nonnegative definite \(2\times2\) matrix.  Then the
distribution of \(T_z\) is continuous in the following cases:
\[
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma=2,
\qquad\text{or}\qquad
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma=1
\text{ and }
\operatorname{Range}\Sigma\ne \operatorname{span}\{n_z\}.
\]
If \(\operatorname{rank}\Sigma=1\) and
\(\operatorname{Range}\Sigma=\operatorname{span}\{n_z\}\), then
\[
\PP(T_z=0)=\frac12
\]
and \(T_z\) has no atom on \((0,\infty)\).  More precisely,
\[
T_z\sim \frac12\delta_0+\frac12\chi^2_1 .
\]
Equivalently,
\[
\PP(T_z=0)=\frac12,\qquad
\PP(T_z\le t)=\frac12+\frac12F_{\chi^2_1}(t),\quad t>0.
\]
Consequently, for a lower quantile level \(0<p_c<1\), the lower
\(p_c\)-quantile is
\[
c_{p_c}=0\quad\text{if }p_c\le\frac12,\qquad
c_{p_c}=F_{\chi^2_1}^{-1}(2p_c-1)\quad\text{if }p_c>\frac12,
\]
and the positive-quantile case is a continuity point of the law of \(T_z\).  If
\(\Sigma=0\), then
\(T_z=0\) almost surely.  Consequently the continuity condition in
\Cref{lem:projected-gaussian-bootstrap-halfspace-main} is automatic except
for the zero covariance case and for the rank-one case in which the retained
Gaussian direction is normal to the exchange-diagonal tangent half-space; in
that rank-one normal case the only discontinuity is the atom at zero.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
If \(\Sigma=0\), the assertion is immediate.  Suppose first that
\(\operatorname{rank}\Sigma=2\).  The Gaussian law has a Lebesgue density on
\(\RR^2\).  On \(H_z\), the statistic is the nonzero quadratic form
\(u^\top\Sigma^+u\).  On \(H_z^c\), the projection is the linear map
\[
L_z u=u-\frac{n_z^\top u}{\|n_z\|^2}n_z
\]
onto the boundary line \(n_z^\top h=0\), and the statistic is the quadratic
form \(u^\top L_z^\top\Sigma^+L_z u\), not identically zero on that half-plane.
For each fixed \(c\), the level set of either nonzero quadratic polynomial has
Lebesgue measure zero.  Hence \(\PP(T_z=c)=0\) for every \(c\).

Now let \(\operatorname{rank}\Sigma=1\).  Write
\(\Sigma=\lambda vv^\top\), where \(\lambda>0\) and \(\|v\|=1\).  Then
\(Z=\xi v\), with \(\xi\sim\mathcal N(0,\lambda)\), and
\[
\Sigma^+=\lambda^{-1}vv^\top .
\]
If \(n_z^\top v=0\), the line
\(\operatorname{span}\{v\}\) lies in the boundary of \(H_z\), so
\(T_z=\xi^2/\lambda\), which has a continuous \(\chi^2_1\) law.  If
\(n_z^\top v\ne0\), one half-line is already in \(H_z\), where again
\(T_z=\xi^2/\lambda\).  On the other half-line,
\[
\Pi_{H_z}(\xi v)=
\xi\left(v-\frac{n_z^\top v}{\|n_z\|^2}n_z\right).
\]
The corresponding coefficient in the seminorm \(\Sigma^+\) is
\[
\lambda^{-1}
\left\{v^\top\left(v-\frac{n_z^\top v}{\|n_z\|^2}n_z\right)\right\}^2 .
\]
This coefficient is positive unless \(v\) is parallel to \(n_z\).  Therefore,
when \(\operatorname{Range}\Sigma\ne\operatorname{span}\{n_z\}\), \(T_z\) is
a two-sided mixture of positive multiples of \(\xi^2\) and has no atoms.  When
  \(\operatorname{Range}\Sigma=\operatorname{span}\{n_z\}\), the outside
  half-line projects to the origin and contributes mass \(1/2\) at zero, while
  the inside half-line gives \(T_z=\xi^2/\lambda\).  Conditional on this
  half-line, \(\xi^2/\lambda\) has the \(\chi^2_1\) law.  Hence the distribution
  is \(\frac12\delta_0+\frac12\chi^2_1\), and the stated lower-quantile split
  follows immediately from its distribution function
  \(F(t)=1/2+\PP\{\chi^2_1\le t\}/2\) for \(t>0\).  This proves the rank-one
  alternatives.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Computable diagonal covariance]\label{lem:dmap-diagonal-covariance-finite-sum-main}\label{prop:computable-diagonal-covariance-main}
Work at a fixed constrained D-MAP exchange-diagonal population point
\(\sigma_z=(2z,z^2)\), \(0<z<1\), equivalently \(p=s=z\).  Put
\[
\theta=-\log z.
\]
Then the diagonal Palm gap law is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-gap-mass-computable-main}
g_k=\PP(G=k)
=z^k\{(1-z)(1+\theta k)-\theta z\},\qquad k\ge0 .
\end{equation}
In particular
\[
g_0=(1-z)-\theta z,\qquad
g_1=z\{(1-z)(1+\theta)-\theta z\}.
\]
Define
\[
\mu=\frac1{1-z}+\frac{\theta z}{(1-z)^2},
\qquad
\nu=\frac{1+z}{(1-z)^2}
     +\frac{\theta z(3+z)}{(1-z)^3},
\qquad
m_2=g_1+g_0^2 .
\]
Then the three inclusion coordinates at this diagonal point are
\[
r(z)=\frac1\mu(1,g_0,m_2)^\top ,
\]
and the quotient denominator is
\[
D(z)=r_1(z)-r_0(z)^2
=-\frac{\theta^2z^2(1-z)^2}{(1-z+\theta z)^2}<0 .
\]
For the cycle reward variables
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-cycle-reward-y-main}
U_j=\left(1,\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},
\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0\}}\right)^\top,
\qquad
L_j=G_j+1,\qquad
Y_j=U_j-r(z)L_j ,
\end{equation}
the \(Y_j\) sequence is one-dependent, centered, and has covariance
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-sigma-r-y-main}
\Sigma_r(z)=\frac1{\mu(z)}
\left[\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)
+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0)\right].
\end{equation}
Every entry in \eqref{eq:diagonal-sigma-r-y-main} is an absolutely convergent
finite or double sum under the explicit masses \eqref{eq:diagonal-gap-mass-computable-main};
indeed the indicators in \(U_j\) restrict the nontrivial terms to
\(G_j\in\{0,1\}\), while the factors containing \(L_j\) are sums of
polynomials in \(k\) against \(g_k=O(kz^k)\).
Let
\[
A_z=
\begin{pmatrix}
1&g_0&m_2\\
g_0&g_0&g_0^2\\
m_2&g_0^2&m_2
\end{pmatrix},
\quad
e_z=
\begin{pmatrix}
\mu\\ g_0\\ 2g_1+g_0^2
\end{pmatrix},
\quad
d_z=
\begin{pmatrix}
\mu\\ \mu g_0\\ \mu g_1+g_0^2
\end{pmatrix},
\]
and
\[
B_z=
\begin{pmatrix}
1&g_0&m_2\\
g_0&g_0^2&g_0m_2\\
m_2&g_0(g_1+g_0)&g_1^2+g_1g_0^2+g_0^3
\end{pmatrix}.
\]
With \(r=r(z)\), the complete-cycle inclusion covariance on the exchange
diagonal is the finite expression
\begin{equation}\label{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-r-finite-main}
\Sigma_r(z)=\frac1\mu\left\{
A_z-e_zr^\top-re_z^\top+\nu rr^\top
+B_z-d_zr^\top+B_z^\top-rd_z^\top
\right\}.
\end{equation}
Consequently the actual diagonal covariance used by the projected-Gaussian
statistic is the reproducible \(2\times2\) matrix
\begin{equation}\label{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main}
\Sigma_\sigma(z)=D\Phi(r(z))\,\Sigma_r(z)\,D\Phi(r(z))^\top
\end{equation}
where \(D\Phi\) is the explicit Jacobian in
\eqref{eq:lambda-gradient-main}--\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main}, evaluated at
the displayed \(r(z)\).  No infinite covariance-tail truncation or simulation
input is hidden in \eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main}: for a fixed
\(z\), all entries are obtained by ordinary arithmetic from
\((z,\theta,g_0,g_1)\), equivalently from the absolutely convergent
finite/double sums in \eqref{eq:diagonal-sigma-r-y-main}.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
On the equal-rate diagonal the gap law is the one in
\eqref{eq:equal-rate-dictionary-main}, which gives
\eqref{eq:diagonal-gap-mass-computable-main}.  Hence
\[
\EE(G+1)=\sum_{k\ge0}\PP(G\ge k)=\mu,\qquad
\EE(G+1)^2=\sum_{k\ge0}(2k+1)\PP(G\ge k)=\nu ,
\]
which gives the displayed closed forms by summing the two elementary geometric
series and their first two derivatives.

For the inclusion-cycle reward in
\Cref{lem:three-inclusion-clt-reduction-main},
\[
U_j=\left(1,\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},
\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0\}}\right)^\top .
\]
Since the gaps are i.i.d., \(\EE U_j=(1,g_0,g_1+g_0^2)^\top\), proving
\(r(z)=\EE U_j/\mu\).  The quotient denominator formula follows by direct
substitution and simplification; it is strictly negative because
\(0<z<1\) and \(\theta>0\).

The matrix \(A_z\) is \(\EE(U_0U_0^\top)\), \(e_z=\EE((G_0+1)U_0)\), and
\(B_z=\EE(U_0U_1^\top)\).  The vector \(d_z=\EE(U_0(G_1+1))\); the only
non-factorized entry is the third component, where
\[
\EE\{(\mathbf1_{\{G_0=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_0=0,G_1=0\}})(G_1+1)\}
=\mu g_1+g_0^2 .
\]
Writing \(Y_j=U_j-r(G_j+1)\), the reward sequence is one-dependent and
\[
\Sigma_r(z)
=\frac1\mu\{\EE(Y_0Y_0^\top)+\EE(Y_0Y_1^\top)+\EE(Y_1Y_0^\top)\}.
\]
Expanding the three expectations gives exactly
\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-r-finite-main}.  The last display is then the
delta-method sandwich for the symmetric quotient coordinates
\(\Phi(r)=(p+s,ps)\), whose Jacobian was computed in
\eqref{eq:lambda-gradient-main}--\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main}.
\end{proof}

\begin{definition}[Certified diagonal covariance branch]\label{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main}
Fix \(0<z<1\) and form the actual D-MAP covariance
\(\Sigma_\sigma(z)\) by \eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main}.  Put
\[
n_z=(-z,1)^\top,\qquad t_z=(1,z)^\top .
\]
For each reported diagonal candidate \(z\), a diagonal branch certificate at
\(z\) is the following finite object.
It contains:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)},leftmargin=2.4em,itemsep=0.2em]
\item an exact description of the input \(z\), or an interval enclosure
\([z]\Subset(0,1)\) if the reported branch is asserted uniformly on that
input interval;
\item an outward-rounded enclosure \([\theta]\) of
\(\theta=-\log z\), or a symbolic identity for \(\theta\);
\item outward-rounded interval enclosures for every scalar arithmetic operation
in \eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-r-finite-main} and
\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main};
\item the three outward-rounded branch quantities
\[
[I_{\tau}]\supset \operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma(z),\qquad
[I_{\det}]\supset \det\Sigma_\sigma(z),\qquad
[w]\supset \Sigma_\sigma(z)t_z,
\]
computed from the enclosed matrix by the directed-rounding formulas in
\Cref{lem:diagonal-interval-branch-certificate-main}.
\end{enumerate}
The certificate transcript printed in the manuscript, or an explicitly
included appendix transcript, must specify the precision, rounding mode, and
the displayed branch margin \(\eta>0\) whenever it asserts a strict positive
inequality.  A repository script may reproduce the transcript, but the
coverage-certified status is attached to the printed interval transcript, not
to an undisplayed local file.  Equalities used to assert a rank-deficient
branch must be exact equalities, either symbolic identities or interval
enclosures of width zero in the certified arithmetic; a small floating-point
residual is not an equality certificate.

The four admissible certified outcomes are:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)},leftmargin=2.4em,itemsep=0.25em]
\item \textbf{Full rank.}  The certificate proves
\[
[I_{\det}]\subset[\eta,\infty).
\]
\item \textbf{Rank one, normal range.}  The certificate proves
\[
[I_{\det}]=[0,0],\qquad
[I_{\tau}]\subset[\eta,\infty),\qquad
[w]=\{(0,0)^\top\}.
\]
\item \textbf{Rank one, non-normal range.}  The certificate proves
\[
[I_{\det}]=[0,0],\qquad
[I_{\tau}]\subset[\eta,\infty),\qquad
[q]\subset[\eta,\infty),
\]
where \([q]\) is the outward-rounded enclosure of
\(\|w\|^2=w_1^2+w_2^2\) obtained from \([w]\).
\item \textbf{Zero covariance.}  The certificate proves
\[
\Sigma_\sigma(z)=0
\]
entrywise, equivalently \(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma(z)=0\) for this
nonnegative definite matrix.
\end{enumerate}
If none of these certificates is available, the diagonal rank branch is
declared inconclusive.  An inconclusive branch may still be used to display the
projected-Gaussian diagnostic \(Q_N(z)\) and the ideal conditional quantile,
but it is not a coverage-certified report in Theorem~E.

The certification convention is deliberately finite.  This paper does not use
an unproved global analytic sign classification of
\(\det\Sigma_\sigma(z)\) on \(0<z<1\), and a floating-point eigenvalue
calculation at a candidate is not a substitute for a certificate.  A
coverage-certified diagonal report may use only a finite certified candidate
domain
\[
Z_{\rm cert}=\{z_1,\ldots,z_J\}
\quad\text{or}\quad
Z_{\rm cert}=\bigcup_{j=1}^J [z_j^-,z_j^+]\Subset(0,1),
\]
where each listed point or interval has its own outward-rounded branch
certificate satisfying one of the four outcomes above, uniformly over the
listed interval when an interval is used.  A candidate outside the displayed
\(Z_{\rm cert}\) is diagnostic only unless an additional certificate is
attached.  The worked certificate in
\Cref{rem:worked-diagonal-branch-certificate-main} supplies the finite
point-domain \(Z_{\rm cert}=\{1/2\}\); no continuum branch classification is
inferred from it.
\end{definition}

\begin{algorithmiclemma}[Outward-rounded diagonal branch certificate]\label{lem:diagonal-interval-branch-certificate-main}
For a fixed point \(0<z<1\), or for a closed input interval
\([z]\Subset(0,1)\), the branch certificate in
\Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main} is reproducibly obtained from
the finite formulas of \Cref{prop:computable-diagonal-covariance-main} as
follows.
Work in an interval arithmetic system with directed rounding.  Enclose the
input by \([z]\), enclose \(\theta=-\log z\) over that whole input enclosure by
\([\theta]\), and then evaluate the displayed formulas
\[
[g_0]=(1-[z])-[\theta][z],
\qquad
[g_1]=[z]\{(1-[z])(1+[\theta])-[\theta][z]\},
\]
\[
[\mu]=\frac1{1-[z]}+\frac{[\theta][z]}{(1-[z])^2},
\qquad
[\nu]=\frac{1+[z]}{(1-[z])^2}
     +\frac{[\theta][z](3+[z])}{(1-[z])^3},
\qquad
[m_2]=[g_1]+[g_0]^2.
\]
Set
\[
[r]=\frac1{[\mu]}(1,[g_0],[m_2])^\top
\]
and form the interval matrices \([A_z]\), \([B_z]\) and interval vectors
\([e_z]\), \([d_z]\) from the displayed formulas immediately preceding
\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-r-finite-main}.  Directed evaluation of
\[
[\Sigma_r]=\frac1{[\mu]}\left\{
[A_z]-[e_z][r]^\top-[r][e_z]^\top+[\nu][r][r]^\top
+[B_z]-[d_z][r]^\top+[B_z]^\top-[r][d_z]^\top
\right\}
\]
encloses the regenerative covariance.
Next compute the quotient-Jacobian interval \([J_\Phi]\) from
\eqref{eq:lambda-gradient-main}--\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main}, with every
division checked to have a denominator interval not containing zero, and put
\[
[\Sigma]=[J_\Phi]\,[\Sigma_r]\,[J_\Phi]^\top .
\]
Writing
\[
[\Sigma]=\begin{pmatrix}
[a]&[b]\\
[c]&[d]
\end{pmatrix},
\]
the branch quantities required by \Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main}
are not optional diagnostics but the certificate data:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-branch-interval-quantities-main}
[I_{\tau}]=[a]+[d],\qquad
[I_{\det}]=[a][d]-[b][c],\qquad
[w]=
\binom{[a]+[z][b]}{[c]+[z][d]},
\qquad
[q]=[w_1]^2+[w_2]^2 .
\end{equation}
If the intervals in \eqref{eq:diagonal-branch-interval-quantities-main}
satisfy one of the four certified outcomes in
\Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main}, then the corresponding
population branch of \(\Sigma_\sigma(z)\) is proved for the fixed candidate,
or uniformly for every candidate in the certified input interval.  If none of
the interval separations succeeds, the branch is reported as inconclusive.
\end{algorithmiclemma}

\begin{proof}
The formulas for \(g_0,g_1,\mu,\nu,m_2,r,A_z,e_z,d_z,B_z\) are exactly the
finite D-MAP covariance formulas of
\Cref{prop:computable-diagonal-covariance-main}.  Directed interval arithmetic
is inclusion isotone for addition, subtraction, multiplication, and division
by an interval excluding zero.  Therefore each displayed interval contains the
corresponding real scalar or matrix entry.  The quotient denominator is
nonzero at every fixed diagonal point by
\eqref{eq:diagonal-quotient-denominator-main}, so sufficiently sharp input
enclosures make the Jacobian divisions certified.  The sandwich product then
contains the real covariance
\(\Sigma_\sigma(z)=D\Phi(r(z))\Sigma_r(z)D\Phi(r(z))^\top\).  Finally,
\eqref{eq:diagonal-branch-interval-quantities-main} is precisely the directed
interval evaluation of
\(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma(z)\), \(\det\Sigma_\sigma(z)\),
\(\Sigma_\sigma(z)(1,z)^\top\), and its squared norm.  Interval exclusion from
zero proves the stated strict inequalities, while a zero-width interval or a
symbolic identity proves the asserted equalities.  The branch conclusion is
then the \(2\times2\) nonnegative-definite rank/range classification of
\Cref{lem:dmap-diagonal-covariance-alternatives-main}.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Worked diagonal branch certificate]\label{rem:worked-diagonal-branch-certificate-main}
The self-contained certificate used by this manuscript is the interval
transcript in \Cref{app:self-contained-diagonal-branch-certificate-main}.  The
paper-local files \path{certificates/diagonal_branch_certificate.py} and
\path{certificates/diagonal_branch_z_half.json} are reproducibility helpers:
they regenerate and machine-check the same transcript, but the theorem-level
branch certificate is the appendix transcript itself.  The command
\path{python certificates/diagonal_branch_certificate.py --test} evaluates the finite covariance and sandwich formulas
\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-r-finite-main}--\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main}
directly from the displayed scalars, matrices and \(D\Phi\), checks that the
stored JSON transcript is exactly the deterministic transcript regenerated by
the script, and verifies that the directed intervals enclose those
independently evaluated printed-formula values.  The unit tests also reject the
older draft trace/determinant intervals as negative regression fixtures; those
rejected numbers are not certificate data and are not used in any branch
decision.  Thus the certificate used below is tied to the printed formulas
rather than to a manually copied numerical table.  The accepted arithmetic is
base-ten decimal interval
arithmetic with precision \(90\) significant digits and directed rounding
downward for every lower endpoint and upward for every upper endpoint; the
input interval and all branch enclosures are printed in
\Cref{app:self-contained-diagonal-branch-certificate-main}.

For this point certificate the exact substitution into
\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-r-finite-main} is
\[
z=\frac12,\qquad
\theta=\log2,\qquad
g_0=\frac{1-\log2}{2},\qquad
g_1=\frac14,
\]
\[
\mu=2+2\log2,\qquad
m_2=g_1+g_0^2 .
\]
Substituting these values in \eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-r-finite-main} and
then in
\(\Sigma_\sigma(z)=D\Phi(r(z))\Sigma_r(z)D\Phi(r(z))^\top\) gives
\[
\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)=
\begin{pmatrix}
25.0645227916081&17.2222593244352\\
17.2222593244352&11.8884649865292
\end{pmatrix}.
\]
\[
\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)
\doteq 36.9529877781373,
\qquad
\det\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)
\doteq 1.37248537400032.
\]
The appendix transcript records outward-rounded enclosures of all these
displayed quantities; in short display form, the corresponding branch
intervals are
\[
\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)
\in[36.95298777813731,36.95298777813732],
\]
\[
\det\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)
\in[1.372485374000321,1.372485374000322].
\]
These two intervals are deliberately rounded outward for compact display; the
full precision determinant, trace, matrix, tangent-vector, rounding-mode, and
input intervals are part of the manuscript in
\Cref{app:self-contained-diagonal-branch-certificate-main}.  The determinant
enclosure is separated from zero, so the certificate proves the full-rank
branch with margin, for instance, \(\eta=1.37\).  The same transcript
also records the outward-rounded vector enclosure for
\(\Sigma_\sigma(1/2)(1,1/2)^\top\); it is not needed for the full-rank
decision but is included because the branch protocol always records the
tangent-direction test.
This is a point certificate only: it certifies the full-rank branch for the
single reported candidate \(z=1/2\), not for neighbouring \(z\)'s and not for
the continuum \(0<z<1\).
\end{remark}

\begin{lemma}[D-MAP diagonal covariance alternatives]\label{lem:dmap-diagonal-covariance-alternatives-main}
Work at a fixed constrained D-MAP exchange-diagonal population point
\(\sigma_z=(2z,z^2)\), \(0<z<1\), and form the actual diagonal covariance
\(\Sigma_\sigma(z)\) by the finite formula
\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main}.  Let \(n_z=(-z,1)^\top\).  Exactly
one of the following mutually exclusive
alternatives holds:
\[
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma_\sigma(z)=2,
\]
\[
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma_\sigma(z)=1
\quad\text{and}\quad
\operatorname{Range}\Sigma_\sigma(z)\ne\operatorname{span}\{n_z\},
\]
\[
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma_\sigma(z)=1
\quad\text{and}\quad
\operatorname{Range}\Sigma_\sigma(z)=\operatorname{span}\{n_z\},
\]
or
\[
\Sigma_\sigma(z)=0 .
\]
The first two alternatives have a continuous projected-Gaussian statistic and
therefore supply exact-level projected-Gaussian cases for all confidence
levels.  In the third alternative the limiting statistic has law
\(\frac12\delta_0+\frac12\chi^2_1\): its distribution function is
\(1/2+F_{\chi^2_1}(t)/2\) for \(t>0\).  Hence its lower \(p_c\)-quantile is
zero for \(p_c\le1/2\), but for \(p_c>1/2\) it is
\(F_{\chi^2_1}^{-1}(2p_c-1)\) and is continuous.  The
fourth alternative has an identically zero limiting statistic.  The occurrence
of the covariance alternative is not a modelling choice: it is obtained by
evaluating the finite matrix
\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main}.  The paper does not prove a
closed-form analytic sign classification of this determinant for every
\(0<z<1\).  Therefore the operational diagonal coverage branches in
Theorem~E are conditional on the certified population branch in
\Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main}, operationally generated by
\Cref{lem:diagonal-interval-branch-certificate-main}, not on a global
closed-form \(z\)-classification and not on an uncertified floating-point rank
label.  In
the rank-one-normal covariance
alternative, the theorem-level branch still depends on the requested quantile
level \(p_c=1-\alpha\): exact unbuffered coverage for \(p_c>1/2\),
and buffered conservative coverage for \(p_c\le1/2\).

Equivalently, because \(\Sigma_\sigma(z)\) is \(2\times2\) and nonnegative
definite, these cases are checked directly from the actual D-MAP covariance by
\[
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma_\sigma(z)=2
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\det\Sigma_\sigma(z)>0,
\]
\[
\Sigma_\sigma(z)=0
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma(z)=0,
\]
and, with \(t_z=(1,z)^\top\) tangent to the exchange diagonal,
\[
\operatorname{Range}\Sigma_\sigma(z)=\operatorname{span}\{n_z\}
\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
\det\Sigma_\sigma(z)=0,\quad
\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma(z)>0,\quad
\Sigma_\sigma(z)t_z=0 .
\]
The remaining case
\[
\det\Sigma_\sigma(z)=0,\quad
\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma(z)>0,\quad
\Sigma_\sigma(z)t_z\ne0
\]
is the rank-one non-normal exact-level alternative.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The matrix \(\Sigma_\sigma(z)\) is a \(2\times2\) nonnegative definite matrix
because it is obtained by sandwiching the nonnegative definite regenerative
covariance \(\Sigma_r(z)\) with the deterministic Jacobian \(D\Phi(r(z))\).
Thus its rank is \(0\), \(1\), or \(2\).  In rank one its range is a single
line, which is either the normal line \(\operatorname{span}\{n_z\}\) or is not
that line.  These alternatives are exhaustive and mutually exclusive.

For a \(2\times2\) nonnegative definite matrix, full rank is equivalent to
positive determinant, and the zero matrix is equivalent to zero trace.  In the
remaining rank-one case, \(\det\Sigma_\sigma(z)=0\) and
\(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma(z)>0\).  Since
\(t_z=(1,z)^\top\) spans the tangent line orthogonal to \(n_z=(-z,1)^\top\),
the range is \(\operatorname{span}\{n_z\}\) exactly when the kernel is the
tangent line, equivalently \(\Sigma_\sigma(z)t_z=0\).  If this kernel test
fails, the rank-one range is not the normal line.  The statements about
continuity, the half-mass at zero, and the zero statistic are precisely
\Cref{thm:projected-gaussian-continuity-degeneracy-main} applied to
\(\Sigma=\Sigma_\sigma(z)\).  The certificate definition is only the same
case analysis written as a reproducible branch-selection rule: each positive
inequality gives the displayed margin, and each asserted rank deficiency is
accepted only when the corresponding equality has been proved exactly.
\end{proof}

\begin{algorithmiclemma}[Finite interval-cover diagonal branch certification]\label{lem:diagonal-certified-interval-cover-main}
Let \(I=[z^-,z^+]\Subset(0,1)\) be a compact diagonal candidate interval that
is reported as coverage-certified.  Algorithm~X is the following deterministic
branch verifier.

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\arabic*)},leftmargin=2.4em,itemsep=0.25em]
\item Start with the queue containing \(I\) and with an empty certified list
\(\mathcal C\).
\item Remove one interval \(J\) from the queue.  Apply the outward-rounded
evaluation in \Cref{lem:diagonal-interval-branch-certificate-main} on the
whole interval \(J\).
\item If exactly one of the four branch tests in
\Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main} succeeds on \(J\), append
\((J,\mathrm{branch})\) to \(\mathcal C\).
\item If no branch test succeeds and the prescribed maximum subdivision depth,
precision budget, or exact-symbolic fallback has not been exhausted, bisect
\(J\) and return the two children to the queue.
\item If no branch test succeeds after the declared resources are exhausted,
append \(J\) to the undecided list \(\mathcal U\).
\end{enumerate}

The transcript accepted by the manuscript contains the input interval \(I\),
the precision and rounding mode, every terminal interval, the branch label and
margin for every interval in \(\mathcal C\), the undecided list
\(\mathcal U\), and an exact rational endpoint check proving
\[
I=\bigcup_{(J,\mathrm{branch})\in\mathcal C}J\ \cup\
\bigcup_{J\in\mathcal U}J
\]
with disjoint interiors.  The interval \(I\) is a reported certified candidate
interval only when this verifier returns a finite terminal list and
\(\mathcal U=\varnothing\).  In that case
\[
I=\bigcup_{j=1}^M I_j
\]
and directed interval evaluation proves exactly one branch condition on each
\(I_j\).  Therefore every candidate \(z\in I\) belongs to a certified
subinterval on which the population branch of
\(\Sigma_\sigma(z)\) is fixed.  If \(\mathcal U\ne\varnothing\), the union of
the undecided intervals is displayed and those candidates are diagnostic only;
they are not in \(Z_{\rm cert}\).
\end{algorithmiclemma}

\begin{proof}
All branch assertions made on a terminal interval are consequences of
\Cref{lem:diagonal-interval-branch-certificate-main}, so each accepted
terminal interval has a proved population branch for every \(z\) in that
interval.  The four branch outcomes in
\Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main} are mutually exclusive for the
actual nonnegative definite matrix \(\Sigma_\sigma(z)\) by
\Cref{lem:dmap-diagonal-covariance-alternatives-main}.  Hence two distinct
branch labels cannot both be valid at the same population point; the verifier
therefore accepts a terminal interval only when its directed enclosures prove a
single outcome uniformly over the interval.

The equality of the endpoint union is a finite rational check in the transcript,
independent of floating-point arithmetic.  When the undecided list is empty,
the accepted terminal intervals cover all of \(I\), and every point in \(I\)
inherits the unique branch proved on the terminal interval containing it.  If
the list is nonempty, no coverage-certified theorem is invoked on that
undecided subunion, which is exactly the reporting convention in
\Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main}.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Model verification.}
We now specialize the generic renewal tools to the standing record experiment
of \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}.  The exponential-tail lemma
verifies the renewal hypothesis for this model.  Theorem~C then states the
exact finite-record statistic for the three inclusion coordinates and applies
the generic complete-cycle CLT to that statistic.

\begin{lemma}[Killed-leakage gap exponential moment]\label{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main}
Under \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}, set
\[
r=\max\{p,s\}<1 .
\]
If \(p\ne s\), then there is a finite constant \(C\), depending only on the
fixed constrained D-MAP setting, such that
\[
g_k\le Cr^k\qquad(k\ge0).
\]
If \(p=s=r\), then there is a finite constant \(C\), depending only on the
fixed constrained D-MAP setting, such that
\[
g_k\le C(1+k)r^k\qquad(k\ge0).
\]
Equivalently, in all cases, for every \(\bar r\in(r,1)\) there is a finite
constant \(C_{\bar r}\) such that
\[
g_k\le C_{\bar r}\bar r^k\qquad(k\ge0).
\]
Consequently, for every
\[
0<\eta<-\log r,
\]
the gap and cycle length satisfy
\[
\EE e^{\eta G}<\infty,\qquad
\EE e^{\eta (G+1)}<\infty .
\]
Thus the regenerative exponential-moment hypothesis used below is a verified
consequence of the killed-leakage model, not an additional assumption.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
When \(p\ne s\), the displayed unequal-rate formula
\eqref{eq:gap-closed-main} gives
\[
|g_k|
\le
\left|\frac{\kappa_{\mathrm r}(1-p)}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma}\right|p^k
+
\left|\frac{\Gamma(1-s)}{\kappa_{\mathrm r}-\Gamma}\right|s^k
\le C_1(p^k+s^k)
\le 2C_1r^k .
\]
Since \(g_k\ge0\), this is the stated unequal-rate bound.

On the equal-rate diagonal \(p=s=r=e^{-\Gamma\Delta\tau}\), write
\(y=\Gamma\Delta\tau\).  The equal-rate dictionary
\eqref{eq:equal-rate-dictionary-main} gives
\[
g_k=r^k\bigl((1-r)(1+yk)-yr\bigr).
\]
Hence \(|g_k|\le C_2(1+k)r^k\), proving the stated equal-rate bound.

The slack-rate formulation follows from these two bounds.  In the unequal-rate
case \(r^k\le\bar r^k\).  In the equal-rate case, with
\(q=r/\bar r<1\),
\[
(1+k)r^k=(1+k)q^k\bar r^k
\le \left(\sup_{m\ge0}(1+m)q^m\right)\bar r^k,
\]
and the supremum is finite.  Absorbing the displayed finite factors into
\(C_{\bar r}\) gives the claimed envelope.

Finally fix \(0<\eta<-\log r\).  In the equal-rate case,
\[
\EE e^{\eta G}
=\sum_{k\ge0}e^{\eta k}g_k
\le C_2\sum_{k\ge0}(1+k)(re^\eta)^k
\]
is finite because \(re^\eta<1\).  In the unequal-rate case the same argument
uses \(g_k\le Cr^k\) and the geometric series
\(\sum_{k\ge0}(re^\eta)^k\).  Finally,
\(\EE e^{\eta(G+1)}=e^\eta\EE e^{\eta G}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Stationary killed-leakage three-inclusion complete-cycle CLT]\label{cor:three-inclusion-complete-cycle-clt-main}
In the stationary version of \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}, let
\(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\) be the exact finite-record statistic from
\Cref{def:cycle-index-inclusion-estimator-main}:
\[
\widehat r_{m,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
=\frac1N\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}U_j^{(m)},\qquad m=0,1,2,
\]
with empty sums equal to \(0\).  Put
\[
r=(r_0,r_1,r_2)^\top,\qquad
r_m=\PP(A_0=A_m=1),\qquad
\mu=\EE(G+1).
\]
In Palm cycle coordinates,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:inclusion-cycle-reward-identities-main}
U_j^{(0)}=1,\qquad
U_j^{(1)}=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},\qquad
U_j^{(2)}
=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,\ G_{j+1}=0\}},
\end{equation}
and the centered reward is
\[
Y_j=
\bigl(U_j^{(0)}-r_0L_j,\,
U_j^{(1)}-r_1L_j,\,
U_j^{(2)}-r_2L_j\bigr)^\top .
\]
Then \(Y_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\), \(\EE Y_j=0\), and the reward sequence is
\(1\)-dependent.  With
\[
\mathcal T_1^{(r)}
=\Var(Y_0)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0),
\qquad
\Sigma_r=\frac{\mathcal T_1^{(r)}}{\mu},
\]
one has
\[
\sqrt N(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r)
\Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r).
\]
The proof uses the \(O_{\PP}(\log N)\) endpoint replacement and the
renewal random-index replacement from
\Cref{prop:generic-complete-cycle-regenerative-clt-main}; the covariance
scale is exactly \(\mathcal T_1^{(r)}/\mu\).
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
The half-open cycle \([\tau_j,\tau_{j+1})\) contains only the click at
\(\tau_j\), so \(U_j^{(0)}=1\).  A one-step look-ahead is a click exactly
when the next click occurs one sampled step later, so
\(U_j^{(1)}=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}}\).  For lag two, the contributing time must
again be the click at \(\tau_j\); the click two steps later occurs either
when \(G_j=1\), or when \(G_j=0\) and the following gap also has length zero.
This gives the displayed reward identities.
