\Cref{lem:local-window-regenerative-decomposition-main}, applied to
\(Z_n^{(m)}=A_nA_{n+m}\), gives the Palm inversion identity
\[
\EE U_j^{(m)}=r_m\EE L_j,\qquad m=0,1,2.
\]
Thus \(\EE Y_j=0\).  The displayed identities show that \(Y_j\) is a
function only of \((G_j,G_{j+1})\), hence \(1\)-dependent, and
\[
\|Y_j\|\le C(1+G_j)
\]
for a fixed finite \(C\).  The exponential moment required by the generic
proposition is supplied by
\Cref{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main}.

For each coordinate,
\[
\widehat r_{m,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r_m
=\frac1N\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}\{U_j^{(m)}-r_mL_j\}
+\frac{r_m}{N}\left\{\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}L_j-N\right\}.
\]
The complete cycles used by \(J_{m,N}\) differ from the calendar window
\([0,N)\) by only the initial incomplete cycle, the terminal incomplete
cycle, and a fixed look-ahead cycle.  Their total length is
\(O_{\PP}(\log N)\), by the endpoint proof in
\Cref{prop:generic-complete-cycle-regenerative-clt-main}.  Therefore the
second displayed term is \(o_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), and the first term has the
same \(N^{1/2}\)-limit as \(N^{-1}\sum_{j=0}^{K_N-1}Y_j\).
\Cref{prop:generic-complete-cycle-regenerative-clt-main} then supplies the
deterministic \(1\)-dependent CLT, the endpoint replacement, and the
random-index replacement \(K_N/N\to1/\mu\).  The resulting calendar-time
covariance is \(\mathcal T_1^{(r)}/\mu\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[CLT reduction for inclusion coordinates]\label{lem:three-inclusion-clt-reduction-main}
Under \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}, let
\(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\), \(K_N\), \(L_j=G_j+1\), \(U_j^{(m)}\),
\(r=(r_0,r_1,r_2)^\top\), and \(\mu=\EE L_0\) be as in
\Cref{cor:three-inclusion-complete-cycle-clt-main}.  Define
\[
Y_j=
\bigl(U_j^{(0)}-r_0L_j,\,
U_j^{(1)}-r_1L_j,\,
U_j^{(2)}-r_2L_j\bigr)^\top
=f(G_j,G_{j+1}),
\]
where
\[
U_j^{(0)}=1,\qquad
U_j^{(1)}=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},\qquad
U_j^{(2)}
=\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0\}} .
\]
Then
\[
\EE Y_j=0,\qquad (Y_j)_{j\in\mathbb Z}\ \text{is \(1\)-dependent},
\qquad
\frac{K_N}{N}\xrightarrow{\PP}\frac1\mu,
\]
and the finite-record statistic has the cycle-sum reduction
\begin{equation}\label{eq:three-inclusion-clt-reduction-main}
\sqrt N(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r)
=
\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_{j=0}^{K_N-1}Y_j+o_{\PP}(1).
\end{equation}
Consequently, with
\[
T_1^{(r)}
=\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)
 +\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0),
\qquad
\Sigma_r=\frac{T_1^{(r)}}{\mu},
\]
one has
\[
\sqrt N(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r)
\Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r).
\]
The same reduction and limit hold for a finite hidden start after a
post-burn-in block of length \(L_N\to\infty\), with \(N\) interpreted as the
retained block length and with all cycle indices formed from that retained
word.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The Palm reward identities are exactly
\eqref{eq:inclusion-cycle-reward-identities-main}.  Palm inversion for the
local windows gives \(\EE U_j^{(m)}=r_m\EE L_j\), hence \(\EE Y_j=0\).  The
displayed formulas make \(Y_j\) a function of the two adjacent gaps
\((G_j,G_{j+1})\), so independent renewal gaps imply \(1\)-dependence.

It remains to isolate the calendar-window error.  For each coordinate,
\[
\widehat r_{m,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r_m
=
\frac1N\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}\{U_j^{(m)}-r_mL_j\}
+\frac{r_m}{N}\left\{\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}L_j-N\right\}.
\]
The complete cycles entering \(J_{m,N}\) differ from the prefix
\(\{0,\ldots,K_N-1\}\) only by the initial incomplete cycle, the terminal
incomplete cycle, and the fixed look-ahead cycles needed by the local
windows.  By the endpoint estimate in
\Cref{prop:generic-complete-cycle-regenerative-clt-main} and the exponential
gap moment of \Cref{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main}, the total
omitted length is \(O_{\PP}(\log N)\).  Therefore both the length correction
and the replacement of \(J_{m,N}\) by the common cycle prefix are
\(o_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), which proves
\eqref{eq:three-inclusion-clt-reduction-main}.

The renewal law of large numbers gives \(K_N/N\to1/\mu\).  Applying the
fixed-\(1\)-dependent multivariate CLT to the deterministic cycle prefix and
then the random-index replacement in
\Cref{prop:generic-complete-cycle-regenerative-clt-main} gives the displayed
Gaussian limit with covariance \(T_1^{(r)}/\mu\).

For a finite hidden start, apply
\Cref{lem:finite-hidden-start-path-contraction-main} with \(L=L_N\).  The
retained cycle-index estimator, the retained complete-cycle count, and the
retained cycle-sum approximation are measurable functions of the retained
visible \(N\)-word.  Hence their joint law under the finite-start protocol
differs from the stationary-start retained-word law by at most
\(C\theta^{L_N}\to0\) in total variation, uniformly in \(N\).  The stationary
\(o_{\PP}(1)\) reduction, the convergence \(K_N/N\to1/\mu\), and the Gaussian
limit therefore transfer to the finite-start retained block.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Local reach and tie-broken projection at the exchange diagonal]\label{lem:exchange-diagonal-local-reach-before-main}
Let
\[
F=\{(x+y,xy):0\le x,y\le1\},\qquad
\sigma_z=(2z,z^2),\quad 0<z<1.
\]
There is an \(\varepsilon_z>0\) such that Euclidean projection onto the full
feasible set \(F\) is single-valued on \(B_{\varepsilon_z}(\sigma_z)\).  On
this ball the deterministic global tie-broken projection
\(\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\) of
\Cref{lem:feasible-set-computable-projection-main} agrees with that unique
ordinary projection, and with the unique local projection onto the hypograph
of the parabola \(\xi_2=\xi_1^2/4\) near \(\sigma_z\).

Consequently, if \(Y_N=\sigma_z+O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), then
\[
\PP\{Y_N\in B_{\varepsilon_z}(\sigma_z)\}\to1
\]
and, with probability tending to one,
\[
\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(Y_N)=\Pi_F(Y_N)=\Pi_{F,z}^{\rm loc}(Y_N),
\]
where \(\Pi_{F,z}^{\rm loc}\) denotes the unique nearest point inside the
local tubular neighbourhood of the exchange arc.  In the same neighbourhood,
for every fixed \(h\in\mathbb R^2\),
\[
N^{1/2}\{\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\sigma_z+h/\sqrt N)-\sigma_z\}
\longrightarrow
\Pi_{H_z}h,\qquad H_z=\{u:u_2\le zu_1\}.
\]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Choose \(\delta>0\) so small that \(2z-\delta>0\), \(2z+\delta<2\), and the
box-boundary pieces of \(F\) other than the exchange parabola are outside
\(B_\delta(\sigma_z)\).  By
\Cref{lem:feasible-set-computable-projection-main}, after possibly shrinking
\(\delta\),
\[
F\cap B_\delta(\sigma_z)
=\{(\xi_1,\xi_2)\in B_\delta(\sigma_z):\xi_2\le \xi_1^2/4\}.
\]
The boundary arc
\(\Gamma_z=\{(t,t^2/4): |t-2z|<\delta\}\) is \(C^2\), hence has a positive
local tubular radius.  Inside this tube the nearest point on \(\Gamma_z\) is
unique, and points on the hypograph side are already feasible.  Since the
closed remote set \(F\setminus B_\delta(\sigma_z)\) has positive distance from
a smaller ball about \(\sigma_z\), one can choose
\(\varepsilon_z>0\) so that no remote feasible point can tie the local nearest
point for any \(v\in B_{\varepsilon_z}(\sigma_z)\).  Thus projection onto the
full, globally nonconvex set \(F\) is single-valued on this ball.  The
tie-broken map \(\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\) is a nearest-point selection; where the
nearest point is unique, its tie-break is inactive, giving the displayed
agreement.

The probability statement follows immediately from
\(\|Y_N-\sigma_z\|=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\).  For
\(v=\sigma_z+h/\sqrt N\), the local feasibility inequality is
\[
z^2+\frac{h_2}{\sqrt N}
\le
\frac{(2z+h_1/\sqrt N)^2}{4},
\]
or
\[
h_2\le zh_1+\frac{h_1^2}{4\sqrt N}.
\]
Thus the scaled local feasible sets converge on bounded \(h\)-sets to the
closed half-space \(H_z=\{h:h_2\le zh_1\}\).  Shapiro's boundary projection
differentiability theorem for sets with positive local reach
\cite{Shapiro1987MetricProjection}, equivalently elementary
Painleve--Kuratowski convergence to the closed convex half-space with unique
nearest points, gives the directional projection limit.  The preceding
global--local agreement transfers this limit from the local projection to
\(\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{assumption}[Finite-record master assumption]\label{ass:finite-record-master-main}
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main,thm:finite-record-quotient-delta-main,thm:finite-record-exchange-diagonal-main}
are read under \Cref{conv:paper-scope-reporting-main} and the following named
hypotheses.  These hypotheses collect the finite-record law, estimand,
reporting gate, covariance-estimation, projection, and diagonal calibration
rules; the theorem statements below invoke these names instead of repeating
the full scope contract.
The algorithmic recipes implementing these conventions are collected after
Theorem~E so that the assumption block is the single pre-theorem contract.

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{FR-\arabic*.},ref=FR-\arabic*,leftmargin=3.0em,itemsep=0.45em]
\item\label{fr:law-main}\textbf{Record law and retained word.}
Assumption~\ref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main} holds.  The observed
data are the retained post-burn-in visible word of length \(N\).  All
complete-cycle sets, batches, projections, Wald statistics, and confidence
sets are functions only of that retained word.

\item\label{fr:estimands-main}\textbf{Visible-record estimands.}
The inclusion estimand is
\[
r=(r_0,r_1,r_2),\qquad r_m=\PP(A_0=A_m=1).
\]
On the stable quotient chart \(r_0>0\) and \(D=r_1-r_0^2\ne0\), the symmetric
quotient estimand is
\[
\sigma_{\rm sym}=\Phi(r)=(p+s,ps),
\qquad
\lambda=\frac{r_2-r_0^2}{r_1-r_0^2}.
\]
The finite visible record identifies \(\{p,s\}\), the unordered numerical rate
set
\[
\left\{-\frac{\log p}{\Delta\tau},-\frac{\log s}{\Delta\tau}\right\},
\]
and, on the exchange diagonal \(p=s=z\), the common numerical rate
\(-\log z/\Delta\tau\).  Ordered labelled rates are not visible-record
estimands here; they require the separate side-data convention in
\Cref{cor:side-data-labelled-rate-wald-main}.

\item\label{fr:protocol-main}\textbf{Operational gate and deterministic thresholds.}
The deterministic thresholds are
\begin{equation}\label{prot:finite-record-main}
b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor,\qquad
t_N=N^{-1/4},\qquad
\varepsilon_N=N^{-1/4},\qquad
\eta_N=N^{-1/8},\qquad
\delta_N=N^{-1/8},
\end{equation}
and the rank-one diagonal angle threshold used in the reporting algorithm is
\(a_N=N^{-1/8}\).  Fix once and for all an integer \(K_{\min}\ge2\).  With
\(K_N=\max\{k:\tau_k<N\}\), with \(K_N=-1\) if no click is observed, with
\(M_N\) the separated-batch count in Algorithm~1, and with
\[
\widehat D_N=
\widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}-(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}})^2,
\]
the reporting event is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-record-reporting-event-main}
\mathcal E_N=
\{K_N\ge K_{\min},\ M_N\ge2,\ \widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\ge\varepsilon_N,
|\widehat D_N|\ge\varepsilon_N\}.
\end{equation}
On \(\mathcal E_N\) the protocol computes the estimator, sandwich covariance,
rank, and any confidence set specified by the applicable theorem branch.  On
\(\mathcal E_N^c\) it reports insufficient complete cycles and forms no
confidence set.  The event is a finite-record reporting gate, not a
population-level exclusion; its high-probability validity on the stable
quotient chart is proved separately in
\Cref{lem:finite-record-reporting-gate-main}.  The separated-batch covariance
rate is \(N^{-1/3}\), the retained-rank threshold is larger than the
covariance-estimation error, \(N^{1/2}\delta_N^2\to\infty\), and
\(t_N/a_N\to0\).

\item\label{fr:covariance-main}\textbf{Covariance estimator and retained rank.}
Algorithm~1 is the covariance rule.  On \(\mathcal E_N\) it reports
\(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}\); on \(\mathcal E_N^c\) it withholds the covariance
matrix and no confidence set is formed.  Probability-limit statements use only
the auxiliary total-space completion
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}
:=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N}\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
+\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{3\times3},
\]
which is not an operational replacement.  The quotient covariance used on the
reported event is the sandwich
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}
=D\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})
 \widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
 D\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})^\top,
\]
after the same feasible projection and chart retention as Algorithm~2.
For full-space probability limits its auxiliary completion is
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{\rm tot}
:=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N}\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}
+\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{2\times2}.
\]
Diagonal statistics use exactly the empirical eigenvalues of
\(\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}\) retained above \(t_N=N^{-1/4}\).  The consistency
needed for that retained-rank step is not assumed here: it is supplied for
the inclusion covariance by \Cref{thm:finite-record-covariance-main} and, on
the exchange diagonal, pushed through the quotient Jacobian in
\Cref{lem:dmap-diagonal-raw-quotient-clt-main}.

\item\label{fr:projection-main}\textbf{Feasible projection and chart retention.}
Algorithm~2 is the projection and chart-retention rule.  The raw symmetric
coordinate is projected to
\[
\widetilde\sigma_N=\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})),
\qquad
F=\{(x+y,xy):0\le x,y\le1\}.
\]
At separated roots with \(\Delta_\sigma>0\), the theorem reports only the
unordered root and rate sets.  A labelled separated-rate report is withheld
unless an external branch map satisfies
\Cref{conv:branch-map-labelled-rates-main}.

\item\label{fr:diagonal-main}\textbf{Exchange-diagonal local projection.}
At \(\sigma_z=(2z,z^2)\), \(0<z<1\), the local-reach hypothesis is exactly
\Cref{lem:exchange-diagonal-local-reach-before-main}: with probability tending
to one, the global tie-broken projection agrees with the unique local
projection, and its tangent projection is onto
\[
H_z=\{h\in\mathbb R^2:h_2\le zh_1\}.
\]

\item\label{fr:mc-main}\textbf{Diagonal calibration and conservative reporting.}
The exchange-diagonal statistic uses the \(t_N\)-thresholded pseudoinverse,
the ideal conditional projected-Gaussian lower quantile
\(c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)\), evaluated analytically or by a deterministic
certified numerical integration routine whenever a coverage-certified
finite-record confidence set is reported.
The theorem itself is the sole coverage statement: before a diagonal candidate
is reported as coverage-certified, the actual computable D-MAP covariance
\(\Sigma_\sigma(z)\) from \eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main} must have
a branch certificate in the sense of
\Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main}.  The certified branch is then
the full-rank/non-normal rank-one exact case, the rank-one-normal case split by
the quantile level \(1-\alpha\), or the zero-covariance conservative case.  If
the certificate is inconclusive, the diagonal output is a projected-Gaussian
diagnostic only and is not a theorem-level confidence report.
Finite Monte Carlo draws from the retained projected-Gaussian law are
coverage-certified only when accompanied by a theorem-level one-sided
simulation certificate.  The canonical certificate used below is the DKW
certificate in \Cref{lem:conditional-monte-carlo-quantile-main}: choose
\(\delta_N\downarrow0\) and \(R_N\to\infty\) with
\[
\varepsilon_{R_N,\delta_N}
=\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta_N)}{2R_N}}<\alpha
\]
eventually, retain the usual record-level reporting event \(\mathcal E_N\) and
the population branch certificate, and replace the raw empirical
\((1-\alpha)\)-quantile by the empirical
\((1-\alpha+\varepsilon_{R_N,\delta_N})\)-quantile.  Equivalently, the
one-sided inflation is the observed gap between these two empirical quantiles.
Without this or another stated domination certificate, the simulated order
statistic is only a diagnostic for the ideal conditional quantile.
\end{enumerate}
\end{assumption}

\begin{lemma}[Finite-record reporting gate is asymptotically open]\label{lem:finite-record-reporting-gate-main}
Work under \Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main},
\ref{fr:law-main}, \ref{fr:estimands-main}, and \ref{fr:protocol-main}.
At any positive constrained D-MAP population point of
\Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}, write
\[
r_0=\rho,\qquad r_1=\rho a,\qquad D:=r_1-r_0^2=\rho(a-\rho).
\]
Then \(r_0=\rho>0\) and \(D=\rho(a-\rho)<0\).
For the reporting event \(\mathcal E_N\) in
\eqref{eq:finite-record-reporting-event-main},
\[
\PP(\mathcal E_N^c)\to0 .
\]
More explicitly,
\[
\PP(K_N<K_{\min})\to0,\qquad
\PP(M_N<2)\to0,
\]
and
\[
\PP(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}<\varepsilon_N)\to0,\qquad
\PP(|\widehat D_N|<\varepsilon_N)\to0 .
\]
The same conclusions hold in the finite hidden-start experiment after any
deterministic burn-in \(L_N\to\infty\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
The D-MAP model identities are those of
\Cref{lem:stationary-main,thm:toeplitz_image_inverse_duality}:
\(r_0=\rho\), \(r_1=\rho a\), and
\[
D=r_1-r_0^2=\rho(a-\rho).
\]
At a positive constrained D-MAP point, \(\rho>0\) and
\(a-\rho<0\), so \(D<0\).  Thus the denominator test below is a consequence
of the population law, not an additional finite-record assumption.

The fixed low-cycle assertion is exactly
\Cref{lem:fixed-low-cycle-event-main}, with \(C=K_{\min}\).  Since
\(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\) and \(d_N=b_N+1=o(N)\), the all-complete-cycle
separated-batch count satisfies \(M_N\ge2\) with probability tending to one by
\Cref{lem:separated-batch-covariance-estimator-main}; equivalently, the
renewal law gives
\[
K_N/N\to\rho=1/\mu>0
\]
in probability, so \(K_N\ge K_{\min}\) with probability tending to one, and
the retained separated-batch count satisfies
\[
M_N\sim K_N/(b_N+1)
\]
up to fixed endpoint and look-ahead deletions.  With
\(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\), this implies \(M_N\to\infty\) in probability,
hence \(M_N\ge2\) with probability tending to one.

\Cref{lem:three-inclusion-clt-reduction-main} gives
\(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), hence
\(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\to r_0\) in probability.  Because
\(r_0>0\) and \(\varepsilon_N=N^{-1/4}\to0\),
\[
\PP(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}<\varepsilon_N)
\le
\PP(|\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r_0|>r_0/2)
\]
for all sufficiently large \(N\), and the right side tends to zero.

The map \(x\mapsto x_1-x_0^2\) is continuous, so
\[
\widehat D_N
=\widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
 -(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}})^2
\xrightarrow{\PP}
r_1-r_0^2=D=\rho(a-\rho)<0 .
\]
Since \(|D|>0\) and \(\varepsilon_N\to0\),
\[
\PP(|\widehat D_N|<\varepsilon_N)
\le
\PP(|\widehat D_N-D|>|D|/2)
\]
eventually, and this probability tends to zero.  A union bound over the four
complementary gate conditions proves \(\PP(\mathcal E_N^c)\to0\).

For a finite hidden start, \(\mathcal E_N\), \(K_N\), \(M_N\),
\(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\), and \(\widehat D_N\) are measurable functions
of the retained \(N\)-word.  The total-variation transfer in
\Cref{lem:finite-hidden-start-path-contraction-main} therefore carries each
stationary-start probability limit to the post-burn-in retained block.
\end{proof}

\begin{maintheorem}[fixed-setting retained-record inclusion CLT]\label{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main}\label{prop:three-coordinate-vector-batch-main}
Assume the finite-record master assumption
\Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main}, specifically
\ref{fr:law-main}, \ref{fr:estimands-main}, \ref{fr:protocol-main}, and
\ref{fr:covariance-main}.
At positive constrained D-MAP population points, the reporting event
\(\mathcal E_N\) obeys \(\PP(\mathcal E_N^c)\to0\) by
\Cref{lem:finite-record-reporting-gate-main}.
From the retained visible word form the complete-cycle inclusion estimator
\[
\widehat r_{m,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
=\frac1N\sum_{j\in J_{m,N}}U_j^{(m)},\qquad m=0,1,2,
\]
with the empty-sum convention in
\Cref{def:cycle-index-inclusion-estimator-main}, and write
\(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}
=(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}},
  \widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}},
  \widehat r_{2,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}})^\top\).  Let
\[
r=(r_0,r_1,r_2)^\top,\qquad r_m=\PP(A_0=A_m=1).
\]
In Palm cycle coordinates put
\[
U_j=
\bigl(1,\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0\}},
\mathbf1_{\{G_j=1\}}+\mathbf1_{\{G_j=0,G_{j+1}=0\}}\bigr)^\top,
\qquad
L_j=G_j+1,\qquad
Y_j=U_j-rL_j .
\]
Then
\[
\EE Y_j=0,\qquad (Y_j)\text{ is \(1\)-dependent},
\]
and
\[
\sqrt N(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r)
=\frac1{\sqrt N}\sum_{j=0}^{K_N-1}Y_j+o_{\PP}(1).
\]

Consequently
\[
\sqrt N(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r)
\Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r),
\]
where
\[
\Sigma_r=\frac1\mu\left\{\operatorname{Var}(Y_0)
+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_0,Y_1)+\operatorname{Cov}(Y_1,Y_0)\right\},
\qquad \mu=\EE L_0 .
\]
The auxiliary total-space separated-batch matrix
\(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}\) from
\ref{fr:covariance-main} obeys
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3}).
\]
The probability-rate assertion is
\Cref{prop:exact-inclusion-separated-batch-covariance-main} with
\(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\).  That proposition is stated for this exact
\(Y_j=f(G_j,G_{j+1})\), verifies \(\EE Y_j=0\) and the finite fourth moment
from the exponential gap tail, and includes the random calendar prefix, the
endpoint/look-ahead deletion, the empirical batch-mean subtraction, and the
plug-in perturbation
\(\widehat Y_j-Y_j=-(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r)L_j\).
\end{maintheorem}

\begin{proposition}[Proposition 3.51a: pre-diagonal quotient delta method]\label{prop:finite-record-pre-diagonal-quotient-rule-main}\label{prop:pre-diagonal-quotient-delta-before-main}
Let \(\widehat r_N\) be any three-coordinate finite-record estimator.  On the
population inverse domain
\[
r_0>0,\qquad D(r):=r_1-r_0^2\ne0,
\]
define
\[
a(r)=\frac{r_1}{r_0},\qquad
u_2(r)=\frac{r_2}{r_0},\qquad
\lambda(r)=\frac{u_2(r)-r_0}{a(r)-r_0},
\]
and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-inverse-delta-map-main}
\Phi(r)=
\bigl(1-a(r)+\lambda(r),\,
r_0(1-\lambda(r))-a(r)+\lambda(r)\bigr).
\end{equation}
Then \(\Phi\) is \(C^1\) on a neighbourhood of every point in this domain, and
its Jacobian is the one displayed in
\eqref{eq:lambda-gradient-main}--\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main}.  If
\[
\sqrt N(\widehat r_N-r)\Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r),
\]
then
\[
\sqrt N\{\Phi(\widehat r_N)-\Phi(r)\}
\Rightarrow
\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_{\sigma}),\qquad
\Sigma_{\sigma}=D\Phi(r)\Sigma_rD\Phi(r)^\top .
\]
Moreover, if a reported covariance estimator has an auxiliary total-space
completion \(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}\) satisfying
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}=o_{\PP}(t_N),
\qquad t_N\downarrow0,\qquad N^{-1/2}=o(t_N),
\]
then the plug-in sandwich
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{\rm tot}
=\mathbf1_{\mathcal E_N}
D\Phi(\widehat r_N)\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}D\Phi(\widehat r_N)^\top
+\mathbf1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{2\times2}
\]
satisfies
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_{\sigma}\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=o_{\PP}(t_N).
\]
If, in addition, the constrained D-MAP population has
\[
\Phi(r)=\sigma=(\sigma_1,\sigma_2)=(p+s,ps),
\qquad
\Delta_\sigma:=\sigma_1^2-4\sigma_2=(p-s)^2>0,
\]
then the two unordered roots of
\[
z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2=0
\]
are locally \(C^1\) functions of \(\sigma=\Phi(r)\).  The unordered
survival-factor set \(\{p,s\}\) is therefore identified locally from
\(\Phi(r)\), and the unordered numerical rate set follows by applying
\[
z\longmapsto -\frac{\log z}{\Delta\tau}
\]
to the two roots.  No diagonal projection, exchange-diagonal confidence, or
diagonal no-label conclusion is asserted in this proposition.

\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
The only possible poles of the displayed rational formula are \(r_0=0\) and
\(D(r)=r_1-r_0^2=0\).  On the stated domain these poles are absent in a small
neighbourhood of \(r\), so \(\Phi\) is \(C^1\), with the already computed
derivative \eqref{eq:lambda-gradient-main}--\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main}.  The
ordinary multivariate delta method gives the Gaussian limit.

The covariance statement is the same continuous-mapping argument at the
Jacobian level.  The CLT gives \(\widehat r_N-r=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\).  Since
\(D\Phi\) is \(C^1\) on the retained neighbourhood,
\[
\|D\Phi(\widehat r_N)-D\Phi(r)\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2}),
\qquad
\|D\Phi(\widehat r_N)\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O_{\PP}(1).
\]
Submultiplicativity and the triangle inequality give
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\left\|
D\Phi(\widehat r_N)\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}D\Phi(\widehat r_N)^\top
-D\Phi(r)\Sigma_rD\Phi(r)^\top
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}\\
&\le
\|D\Phi(\widehat r_N)\|_{\operatorname{op}}^2
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}\\
&\quad+
\|D\Phi(\widehat r_N)-D\Phi(r)\|_{\operatorname{op}}\,
\|\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}\,
\{\|D\Phi(\widehat r_N)\|_{\operatorname{op}}+\|D\Phi(r)\|_{\operatorname{op}}\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The first term is \(o_{\PP}(t_N)\); the second is \(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), hence
\(o_{\PP}(t_N)\) by the stated threshold scale.

If \(\Delta_\sigma>0\), the discriminant is nonzero and the implicit-function
theorem applied to \(z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2\) gives two local \(C^1\) root
charts; taking the set of their values gives the unordered root map, independent
of a branch labelling.  The logarithm map is \(C^1\) on compact subintervals of
\((0,1)\), so the unordered numerical rate set is the image of this unordered
root set under \(z\mapsto-\log z/\Delta\tau\).

\end{proof}

\begin{maintheorem}[fixed-setting retained-record quotient delta method]\label{thm:finite-record-quotient-delta-main}
Assume the finite-record master assumption
\Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main}, specifically
\ref{fr:law-main}--\ref{fr:projection-main}.  Assume also that the population
inverse is in the stable quotient chart
\[
r_0>0,\qquad D=r_1-r_0^2\ne0 .
\]
Define the quotient map
\[
\Phi(r)=\left(1-\frac{r_1}{r_0}+\lambda,\;
r_0(1-\lambda)-\frac{r_1}{r_0}+\lambda\right),
\qquad
\lambda=\frac{r_2-r_0^2}{r_1-r_0^2}.
\]
Then
\[
\sqrt N\{\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})-\Phi(r)\}
\Rightarrow
\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_\sigma),
\qquad
\Sigma_\sigma=D\Phi(r)\Sigma_rD\Phi(r)^\top.
\]
For the constrained D-MAP population, \(\Phi(r)=(p+s,ps)\).  If
\(\Delta_\sigma=\sigma_1^2-4\sigma_2>0\), the polynomial
\(z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2\) identifies the unordered survival-factor set
\(\{p,s\}\), and hence the unordered numerical rate set
\[
\left\{-\frac{\log p}{\Delta\tau},-\frac{\log s}{\Delta\tau}\right\}.
\]
\end{maintheorem}

\begin{lemma}[Diagonal raw quotient CLT from Theorem C and Proposition 3.51a]\label{lem:dmap-diagonal-raw-quotient-clt-main}
Assume the finite-record master assumption
\Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main}, specifically
\ref{fr:law-main}--\ref{fr:projection-main}.  Fix a constrained D-MAP
exchange-diagonal population point \(p=s=z\), \(0<z<1\), put
\(\theta=-\log z\), and write \(\sigma_z=(2z,z^2)\).  Let \(r(z)\) be the
corresponding inclusion-coordinate vector in
\Cref{prop:computable-diagonal-covariance-main}.  Then the quotient denominator
has the explicit diagonal value
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-quotient-denominator-main}
D(z):=r_1(z)-r_0(z)^2
=-\frac{\theta^2z^2(1-z)^2}{(1-z+\theta z)^2}<0 .
\end{equation}
Consequently the diagonal point is not a singular point of the quotient map
\[
\Phi(r)=\left(1-\frac{r_1}{r_0}+\lambda,\;
r_0(1-\lambda)-\frac{r_1}{r_0}+\lambda\right),
\qquad
\lambda=\frac{r_2-r_0^2}{r_1-r_0^2}.
\]
The map \(\Phi\) is \(C^1\) on a neighbourhood of \(r(z)\), and \(D\Phi\) is
locally bounded there.  Hence, if Theorem~C gives
\[
\sqrt N(\widehat r_N-r(z))\Rightarrow
\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r(z)),
\]
then the raw diagonal symmetric-coordinate estimator
\[
\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}:=\Phi(\widehat r_N)
\]
satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-raw-quotient-clt-main}
\sqrt N\{\Phi(\widehat r_N)-\Phi(r(z))\}
\Rightarrow
\mathcal N\!\left(0,\,
D\Phi(r(z))\,\Sigma_r(z)\,D\Phi(r(z))^\top\right).
\end{equation}
For the constrained D-MAP population, \(\Phi(r(z))=\sigma_z\).

Moreover, for any reported inclusion covariance estimator whose auxiliary
total-space completion satisfies
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r(z)\|_{\operatorname{op}}=o_{\PP}(t_N),
\qquad t_N\downarrow0,\qquad N^{-1/2}=o(t_N),
\]
the reported plug-in sandwich on \(\mathcal E_N\),
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}
=D\Phi(\widehat r_N)\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}D\Phi(\widehat r_N)^\top,\qquad
\Sigma_\sigma(z)=D\Phi(r(z))\Sigma_r(z)D\Phi(r(z))^\top,
\]
has auxiliary total-space completion
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{\rm tot}
:=\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N}\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}
+\mathbf 1_{\mathcal E_N^c}0_{2\times2}
\]
satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-sandwich-consistency-main}
\|\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_\sigma(z)\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=o_{\PP}(t_N).
\end{equation}
In particular, with the separated-batch estimator from
\Cref{thm:finite-record-covariance-main} and \(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\),
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_\sigma(z)\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3})=o_{\PP}(N^{-1/4}).
\]
Thus the raw diagonal CLT and the covariance consistency needed for the
exchange-diagonal projection theorem are consequences of Theorem~C,
\Cref{prop:pre-diagonal-quotient-delta-before-main}, and the separated-batch
covariance theorem, not separate diagonal assumptions and not consequences of
Theorem~D or Theorem~E.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
\Cref{prop:computable-diagonal-covariance-main} gives the diagonal coordinates
\(r(z)\).  Substituting the equal-rate dictionary with
\(\theta=-\log z\) into \(D=r_1-r_0^2\) gives
\eqref{eq:diagonal-quotient-denominator-main}.  Since \(0<z<1\),
\(\theta>0\), \(z>0\), \(1-z>0\), and
\[
1-z+\theta z>0,
\]
the denominator in \eqref{eq:diagonal-quotient-denominator-main} is nonzero
and \(D(z)<0\).  Also \(r_0(z)>0\).  Therefore the rational formula defining
\(\Phi\) has no pole on a small neighbourhood of \(r(z)\), and the derivative
displayed in \eqref{eq:lambda-gradient-main}--\eqref{eq:phi-jacobian-main} is
finite and locally bounded there.

Theorem~C,
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main}, gives the displayed inclusion
CLT for the complete-cycle estimator \(\widehat r_N=\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\)
on the retained reporting branch.  The ordinary \(C^1\) delta method applied
to \(\Phi\), equivalently the diagonal specialization of the pre-diagonal
quotient rule \Cref{prop:pre-diagonal-quotient-delta-before-main}, gives
\eqref{eq:diagonal-raw-quotient-clt-main}.  The population quotient-inverse
identity gives \(\Phi(r(z))=(p+s,ps)=(2z,z^2)=\sigma_z\).

It remains to check that the sandwich covariance consistency is inherited from
the inclusion-coordinate covariance estimator.  The CLT implies
\(\widehat r_N-r(z)=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), hence
\(D\Phi(\widehat r_N)\to D\Phi(r(z))\) in probability; local boundedness gives
\[
\|D\Phi(\widehat r_N)\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O_{\PP}(1),\qquad
\|D\Phi(\widehat r_N)-D\Phi(r(z))\|_{\operatorname{op}}=o_{\PP}(1).
\]
The regenerative covariance \(\Sigma_r(z)\) is a fixed finite matrix.  Using
the triangle inequality and submultiplicativity,
\[
\begin{aligned}
&\left\|
D\Phi(\widehat r_N)\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}D\Phi(\widehat r_N)^\top
-D\Phi(r(z))\Sigma_r(z)D\Phi(r(z))^\top
\right\|_{\operatorname{op}}\\
&\le
\|D\Phi(\widehat r_N)\|_{\operatorname{op}}^2
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r(z)\|_{\operatorname{op}}\\
&\quad+
\|D\Phi(\widehat r_N)-D\Phi(r(z))\|_{\operatorname{op}}\,
\|\Sigma_r(z)\|_{\operatorname{op}}\,
\{\|D\Phi(\widehat r_N)\|_{\operatorname{op}}
+\|D\Phi(r(z))\|_{\operatorname{op}}\}.
\end{aligned}
\]
The first term is \(o_{\PP}(t_N)\) by the assumed inclusion covariance rate.
For the second term, the concrete retained-rank choices in
\Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main} use \(t_N=N^{-1/4}\), and the displayed
hypothesis \(N^{-1/2}=o(t_N)\) is the only rate fact needed here.  The CLT gives
\(\|D\Phi(\widehat r_N)-D\Phi(r(z))\|_{\operatorname{op}}=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\)
because \(D\Phi\) is \(C^1\) on the retained neighbourhood.  Thus the second
term is \(o_{\PP}(t_N)\), proving
\eqref{eq:diagonal-sandwich-consistency-main}.  With
\Cref{thm:finite-record-covariance-main} and
\(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\), the inclusion covariance rate is
\(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3})\), so the displayed \(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/3})=o_{\PP}(N^{-1/4})\)
specialization follows.  The finite covariance formula is exactly
\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main} on \(\mathcal E_N\).  On
\(\mathcal E_N^c\) the auxiliary zero completion is used only in the preceding
probability bound, while the operational protocol reports no covariance matrix.
\end{proof}

\begin{maintheorem}[fixed-candidate exchange-diagonal projected-Gaussian acceptance test]\label{thm:finite-record-exchange-diagonal-main}
Assume the finite-record master assumption
\Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main}, specifically
\ref{fr:law-main}--\ref{fr:mc-main}.  Fix a true exchange-diagonal point
\(\sigma_{z_0}=(2z_0,z_0^2)\), \(0<z_0<1\), and a confidence level
\(0<\alpha<1\).

Let
\[
\widehat\sigma_N=\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}),\qquad
\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}:=\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}),\qquad
\sigma_z=(2z,z^2),\qquad
H_z=\{h\in\mathbb R^2:h_2\le zh_1\}.
\]
By \Cref{lem:dmap-diagonal-raw-quotient-clt-main}, at the true \(z_0\)
\[
\sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N^{\rm raw}-\sigma_{z_0})
\Rightarrow Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)),\qquad
\|\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=o_{\PP}(t_N).
\]
By \Cref{lem:exchange-diagonal-local-reach-before-main}, the global
tie-broken projection agrees with the ordinary local projection near
\(\sigma_{z_0}\) with probability tending to one, and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:diagonal-projected-limit-main}
\sqrt N(\widehat\sigma_N-\sigma_{z_0})
\Rightarrow
\Pi_{H_{z_0}}(Z),\qquad
Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)).
\end{equation}
Equivalently, for every fixed \(h\in\mathbb R^2\),
\[
N^{1/2}\bigl(\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\sigma_z+h/\sqrt N)-\sigma_z\bigr)
\longrightarrow \Pi_{H_z}h .
\]

For each fixed candidate \(0<z<1\), form the retained-rank statistic
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundary-projected-statistic-main}
Q_N(z)=
N(\widehat\sigma_N-\sigma_z)^\top\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{+}
(\widehat\sigma_N-\sigma_z),
\end{equation}
where \(\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{+}\) is the \(t_N\)-thresholded
pseudoinverse.  Let \(c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)\) be the ideal lower conditional
\((1-\alpha)\)-quantile of
\[
T_N^\ast(z)=
\Pi_{H_z}(Z_N^\ast)^\top\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{+}\Pi_{H_z}(Z_N^\ast),
\qquad
Z_N^\ast\mid A_0,\ldots,A_{N-1}\sim
\mathcal N(0,\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}).
\]
The theorem-level coverage objects are the fixed-candidate acceptance events
\begin{equation}\label{eq:boundary-projected-acceptance-event-main}
\mathcal A_N(z)
:=\{Q_N(z)\le c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)\},
\qquad
\mathcal A_N^\varepsilon(z)
:=\{Q_N(z)\le c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)+\varepsilon\},
\quad \varepsilon>0 .
\end{equation}
These events are evaluated at the true but fixed diagonal coordinate \(z_0\).
They are ideal conditional projected-Gaussian events.  A finite report is
coverage-certified only after it attaches a finite certificate bundle:
\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\alph*)},leftmargin=2.2em,itemsep=0.15em]
\item a branch certificate proving \(z_0\in Z_{\rm cert}\) and one of the
population covariance alternatives of
\Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main};
\item in the full-rank branch, a deterministic interval enclosure
\((L,U]\ni c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z_0)\), or an analytic evaluation of the same
ideal conditional quantile, sufficient to decide the reported acceptance
inequality;
\item for any finite Monte Carlo replacement of the ideal critical value, a
one-sided domination certificate
\[
\widehat c^-_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z_0)+d_{R,\delta_N,N}(z_0)
\ge c^-_{1-\alpha,N}(z_0)
\]
with conditional error at most \(\delta_N\).
\end{enumerate}
Without this bundle the theorem supplies the conditional projected-Gaussian
limit and diagnostic ideal-quantile calculation, but no coverage-certified
finite acceptance report.
Let
\[
T_z=(\Pi_{H_z}Z)^\top\Sigma_\sigma(z)^+(\Pi_{H_z}Z),\qquad
c_p(z)=\inf\{c:\PP(T_z\le c)\ge p\},\quad 0<p<1,
\]
with \(Z\sim\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_\sigma(z))\).  The covariance
\(\Sigma_\sigma(z)\) is not an abstract rank assumption: it is the computable
D-MAP matrix in \eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main}.  The theorem
applies only when \(z_0\in Z_{\rm cert}\) and the outward-rounded branch
certificate of
\Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main,lem:diagonal-interval-branch-certificate-main}
certifies the corresponding population alternative.
Put \(p_c=1-\alpha\).
The following list is the theorem-level case split used for reporting.  It is
a split by the pair \((\Sigma_\sigma(z_0),p_c)\), not by the covariance rank
alone: in the rank-one normal covariance alternative the same limiting law
gives an exact positive critical value when \(p_c>1/2\), but gives only the
atom-at-zero buffered branch when \(p_c\le1/2\).

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)},leftmargin=2.5em,itemsep=0.55em]
\item \textbf{Exact projected-Gaussian coverage.}
Suppose a diagonal branch certificate proves one of the following three
alternatives for the computable D-MAP covariance in
\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main}:
\[
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)=2,
\]
or
\[
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)=1,\qquad
\operatorname{Range}\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)\ne
\operatorname{span}\{(-z_0,1)^\top\},
\]
or
\[
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)=1,\quad
\operatorname{Range}\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)=
\operatorname{span}\{(-z_0,1)^\top\},
\quad p_c>1/2 .
\]
Then the lower \((1-\alpha)\)-quantile \(c_{1-\alpha}(z_0)\) is a continuity
point of the law of \(T_{z_0}\), the ideal projected-Gaussian calibration is
exact, and the unbuffered fixed-candidate acceptance event has pointwise
first-order coverage
\[
\PP\{\mathcal A_N(z_0)\}\longrightarrow 1-\alpha .
\]
In the full-rank alternative the quantile is the deterministic number
\[
c_{p_c}(z_0,\Sigma_\sigma(z_0))
=\inf\{c\ge0:F_{z_0,\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)}(c)\ge p_c\},
\]
where \(F_{z,\Sigma}\) is the two-region projected-Gaussian distribution
function constructed in
\Cref{thm:certified-projected-gaussian-quantile-algorithm-main,lem:deterministic-full-rank-projected-gaussian-quantile-main}.  Its
reported ideal-quantile implementation is coverage-certified only when the
finite numerical certificate in
\Cref{thm:certified-projected-gaussian-quantile-algorithm-main,lem:certified-deterministic-projected-gaussian-quantile-main} supplies
an interval \((L,U]\) containing this quantile, after Gaussian tail
truncation, interval quadrature over the two projected regions, and bisection
to a declared stopping tolerance.  In the
rank-one non-normal alternative \(T_{z_0}\) is also continuous by
\Cref{thm:projected-gaussian-continuity-degeneracy-main}.  In the third,
rank-one-normal alternative, put
\[
n_{z_0}=(-z_0,1)^\top,\qquad
\operatorname{Range}\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)=\operatorname{span}\{n_{z_0}\}.
\]
Then
\[
\PP(T_{z_0}=0)=\frac12,\qquad
\PP(T_{z_0}\le t)=\frac12+\frac12F_{\chi^2_1}(t),\quad t>0,
\]
and therefore
\[
c_{p_c}(z_0)=F_{\chi^2_1}^{-1}(2p_c-1)>0
\]
so the lower quantile is positive and the distribution is continuous at that
quantile.  Thus ordinary confidence levels such as \(90\%\) and \(95\%\)
belong to the exact branch even in the rank-one-normal covariance alternative.

\item \textbf{Rank-one normal atom-at-zero branch: buffered lower coverage.}
Suppose a diagonal branch certificate proves that the constrained D-MAP
covariance has the rank-one normal direction
\[
n_{z_0}=(-z_0,1)^\top,\qquad
\operatorname{rank}\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)=1,\qquad
\operatorname{Range}\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)=
\operatorname{span}\{n_{z_0}\}.
\]
If \(p_c\le1/2\), then
\[
\PP(T_{z_0}=0)=\frac12,\qquad
\PP(T_{z_0}\le t)=\frac12+\frac12F_{\chi^2_1}(t),\quad t>0,
\]
and hence
\[
c_{p_c}(z_0)=0,\qquad \PP(T_{z_0}=0)=\frac12,
\]
so the lower quantile lies at the atom.  No exact unbuffered coverage statement
is made from this branch alone.  For every fixed
\(\varepsilon>0\),
\[
\liminf_{N\to\infty}
\PP\{\mathcal A_N^\varepsilon(z_0)\}
\ge1-\alpha .
\]

\item \textbf{Zero covariance: zero-rank fallback and buffered coverage.}
If a diagonal branch certificate proves
\[
\Sigma_\sigma(z_0)=0,
\]
then the covariance consistency supplied by
\Cref{lem:dmap-diagonal-raw-quotient-clt-main} implies that all empirical
eigenvalues are below \(t_N\) with probability
tending to one.  Hence the retained pseudoinverse is zero, \(Q_N(z_0)=0\), and
the conditional projected-Gaussian statistic \(T_N^\ast(z_0)\) is also zero
with probability tending to one.  The protocol reports the retained-rank-zero
fallback, not an exact unbuffered interval.  For every fixed
\(\varepsilon>0\),
\[
\liminf_{N\to\infty}
\PP\{\mathcal A_N^\varepsilon(z_0)\}
\ge1-\alpha .
\]
\end{enumerate}

The conservative lower bounds in (ii) and (iii) are the buffered
random-quantile conclusion of
\Cref{lem:buffered-random-quantile-coverage-main}, applied to
\[
T_N=Q_N(z_0),\qquad T=T_{z_0},\qquad
F_N^\ast(t)=\PP^\ast\{T_N^\ast(z_0)\le t\},\qquad p=1-\alpha .
\]
They do not require continuity of the lower limiting quantile.

Algorithm~3 below is therefore an implementation protocol for the ideal
conditional projected-Gaussian calibration and for its finite-simulation
transfer when both the population branch and the simulation error are
certified.  The ideal fixed-candidate event is
\[
\mathcal A_N(z_0)
=\{Q_N(z_0)\le c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z_0)\}.
\]
The finite-Monte-Carlo event, when reported, is
\[
\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{MC}}(z_0)
:=\{Q_N(z_0)\le
\widehat c^-_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z_0)+d_{R,\delta_N,N}(z_0)\}.
\]
\textbf{Finite-Monte-Carlo coverage transfer.}
One concrete choice is the DKW certificate from
\Cref{lem:conditional-monte-carlo-quantile-main}.  Choose
\(\delta_N\downarrow0\) and \(R_N\to\infty\) so that
\[
\varepsilon_{R_N,\delta_N}
=\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta_N)}{2R_N}}<\alpha
\]
eventually, and set
\[
d_{R_N,\delta_N,N}^{\mathrm{DKW}}(z_0)
=
T_{N,(\lceil(1-\alpha+\varepsilon_{R_N,\delta_N})R_N\rceil)}^\ast(z_0)
-T_{N,(\lceil(1-\alpha)R_N\rceil)}^\ast(z_0).
\]
Then the inflated simulated critical value is
\[
\widehat c^-_{1-\alpha,N,R_N}(z_0)
+d_{R_N,\delta_N,N}^{\mathrm{DKW}}(z_0)
=T_{N,(\lceil(1-\alpha+\varepsilon_{R_N,\delta_N})R_N\rceil)}^\ast(z_0),
\]
and it dominates the ideal lower conditional quantile with conditional
probability at least \(1-\delta_N\), independently of atoms or flat stretches
of the conditional law.  More generally, let
\(G_N=G_{\mathrm{cert},N}(z_0)\) denote the record-level event on which the
candidate is reported and the branch certificate, ideal conditional quantile
enclosure, and numerical recipe are certified.  If any of these displayed
certificate components is absent, the finite output is treated as diagnostic
and \(G_N\) is not asserted for coverage reporting.  Suppose
\[
\PP(G_N)\to1,\qquad \delta_N\to0,
\]
and on \(G_N\),
\[
\PP^\ast\{\widehat c^-_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z_0)
+d_{R,\delta_N,N}(z_0)\ge c^-_{1-\alpha,N}(z_0)\}\ge1-\delta_N .
\]
Then
\[
\PP\{\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{MC}}(z_0)\}
\ge
\PP\{\mathcal A_N(z_0)\}
-\delta_N-\PP(G_N^c)-o(1).
\]
Therefore, if the ideal event has limiting coverage \(1-\alpha\), the simulated
event has
\[
\liminf_N
\PP\{\mathcal A_N^{\mathrm{MC}}(z_0)\}
\ge1-\alpha .
\]
Pushing the fixed diagonal candidate through
\[
z\longmapsto -\frac{\log z}{\Delta\tau}
\]
gives the corresponding pointwise acceptance event for the identified common
numerical rate \(r_{\rm diag}(z_0)\), equivalently for the invariant
ordered quotient pair
\((r_{\rm diag}(z_0),r_{\rm diag}(z_0))\).  The acceptance event is
identical before and after this push-forward.
\end{maintheorem}

\paragraph{Finite-record implementation algorithms for Theorems C--E.}
\phantomsection\label{alg:all-complete-cycle-separated-batch-main}
The following algorithms implement the reporting conventions fixed in
\Cref{ass:finite-record-master-main}.  They are computational protocols, not
additional assumptions.

\medskip
\noindent\fbox{\begin{minipage}{0.965\textwidth}
\textbf{Algorithm 1: all-complete-cycle separated-batch covariance.}

\begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{\arabic*.},leftmargin=2.0em,itemsep=0.35em]
\item From the retained record \(A_0,\ldots,A_{N-1}\), form the complete-cycle
sets \(J_{m,N}\) of
\Cref{def:cycle-index-inclusion-estimator-main} and use only the common set
\[
J_N:=J_{0,N}\cap J_{1,N}\cap J_{2,N}.
\]
Initial incomplete cycles, terminal incomplete cycles, and the fixed terminal
look-ahead cycles required by the local windows are omitted.

\item For each \(j\in J_N\), compute the observable centered reward
\[
\widehat Y_j=
\bigl(U_j^{(0)}-\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}L_j,\,
U_j^{(1)}-\widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}L_j,\,
U_j^{(2)}-\widehat r_{2,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}L_j\bigr)^\top .
\]

\item With \(b_N=\lfloor N^{1/3}\rfloor\), set \(d_N=b_N+1\) and use separated
batch grids
\[
I_{\ell,N}:=\{(\ell-1)d_N,\ldots,(\ell-1)d_N+b_N-1\}.
\]
The omitted index \(\ell d_N-1\) is the separator.

\item Let
\[
M_N:=\max\{m\ge0:\ I_{\ell,N}\subseteq J_N
\text{ for every }1\le\ell\le m\},
\]
with \(M_N=0\) if the set is empty.  A partial terminal batch is omitted.
With \(K_N=\max\{k:\tau_k<N\}\), and with \(K_N=-1\) if no click is observed,
set
\[
\widehat D_N=
\widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}-(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}})^2,
\qquad
\mathcal E_N=
\{K_N\ge K_{\min},\ M_N\ge2,
\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\ge\varepsilon_N,
|\widehat D_N|\ge\varepsilon_N\}.
\]

\item Define
\[
\widehat B_{\ell,N}:=\sum_{j\in I_{\ell,N}}\widehat Y_j,\qquad
\overline{\widehat B}_N:=M_N^{-1}\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}\widehat B_{\ell,N}
\quad\text{on }\mathcal E_N.
\]
Report the covariance estimator only on \(\mathcal E_N\):
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
=
\frac{K_N}{N}\,
\frac1{b_N(M_N-1)}
\sum_{\ell=1}^{M_N}
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)
(\widehat B_{\ell,N}-\overline{\widehat B}_N)^\top .
\]
On \(\mathcal E_N^c\), do not form a zero replacement; report
``insufficient complete cycles; no confidence set.''
\end{enumerate}
\end{minipage}}

\medskip
\noindent\fbox{\begin{minipage}{0.965\textwidth}
\textbf{Algorithm 2: quotient inverse, projection, and chart retention.}

Compute \(\widehat D_N\) and apply the denominator thresholds
\(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\ge\varepsilon_N\) and
\(|\widehat D_N|\ge\varepsilon_N\).  If \(\mathcal E_N^c\) occurs, stop
retained-record inference and report ``insufficient complete cycles; no
confidence set.''  On \(\mathcal E_N\), compute
\(\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})\) and project the resulting raw symmetric
coordinate by
\(\widetilde\sigma_N=\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}))\).
Report separated labelled rates only when
\[
\widehat\Delta_{\sigma,N}\ge\delta_N^2,
\qquad
\widehat p_N,\widehat s_N\in[\eta_N,1-\eta_N],
\]
and the deterministic oracle branch map \(\mathcal B\) is unique on the two
projected roots.  If an estimated branch map is used, require the
high-probability oracle-agreement condition from
\Cref{conv:branch-map-labelled-rates-main}; otherwise withhold separated
labels.  At a population exchange diagonal, use only the pointwise common-rate
acceptance event calibrated by Algorithm~3; a simultaneous diagonal band needs
a separate certified inversion rule.
\end{minipage}}

\medskip
\noindent\fbox{\begin{minipage}{0.965\textwidth}
\textbf{Algorithm 3: projected-Gaussian diagonal calibration and Monte Carlo diagnostics.}

Fix \(0<\alpha<1\), a finite certified candidate domain \(Z_{\rm cert}\) as in
\Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main,lem:diagonal-certified-interval-cover-main},
a candidate diagonal coordinate \(0<z<1\), a Monte Carlo draw count \(R=R_N\)
for optional diagnostics, and \(n_z=(-z,1)^\top\).  The algorithm has two
outputs: an ideal conditional projected-Gaussian calculation and, only when
all certificates below are present, a coverage-certified finite report.  If
\(z\notin Z_{\rm cert}\), continue
only for diagnostic plotting or diagnostic ideal-quantile evaluation and
report ``diagonal candidate not in certified domain; no coverage-certified
acceptance event.''  If \(z\in Z_{\rm cert}\), compute the population diagonal
covariance \(\Sigma_\sigma(z)\) from the finite D-MAP formula
\eqref{eq:dmap-diagonal-sigma-finite-main} and attach the outward-rounded
branch certificate specified in
\Cref{def:dmap-diagonal-rank-certificate-main,lem:diagonal-interval-branch-certificate-main}.
The certificate transcript must display the intervals for
\(\operatorname{tr}\Sigma_\sigma(z)\), \(\det\Sigma_\sigma(z)\), and
\(\Sigma_\sigma(z)(1,z)^\top\).  If the branch certificate is inconclusive,
continue only if a diagnostic projected-Gaussian plot or diagnostic ideal
quantile is desired, and report ``diagonal covariance branch not certified; no
coverage-certified acceptance event.''  A finite-sample eigenvalue calculation of
\(\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}\) is not a substitute for this population
certificate.

Compute
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}
=D\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})
 \widehat\Sigma_{r,N}
 D\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})^\top
\]
on the reporting event \(\mathcal E_N\); if \(\mathcal E_N^c\) occurs, stop
and report ``insufficient complete cycles; no confidence set.''  Symmetrize
the matrix for numerical reporting and truncate only roundoff-negative
eigenvalues to zero.

Diagonalize
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}
=\sum_{i=1}^2\widehat\lambda_{i,N}
\widehat v_{i,N}\widehat v_{i,N}^{\top},
\qquad
\widehat\lambda_{1,N}\ge\widehat\lambda_{2,N}\ge0,
\]
retain exactly
\[
\widehat I_N=\{i:\widehat\lambda_{i,N}>t_N\},
\qquad \widehat k_N=|\widehat I_N|,
\]
and use
\[
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{+}
=\sum_{i\in\widehat I_N}
\widehat\lambda_{i,N}^{-1}
\widehat v_{i,N}\widehat v_{i,N}^{\top}.
\]
If \(\widehat k_N=0\), report ``retained rank zero'' and use only the
buffered conservative acceptance event from Theorem~E when the population
certificate is the zero-covariance branch; otherwise this finite retained-rank
event is an asymptotic pseudoinverse event, not a population branch label.  If
the population certificate is ``rank-one normal direction,'' then, with
\(p_c=1-\alpha\),
the limiting law satisfies
\[
\PP(T_z=0)=\frac12,\qquad
\PP(T_z\le t)=\frac12+\frac12F_{\chi^2_1}(t),\quad t>0.
\]
Thus \(c_{p_c}=0\) for \(p_c\le1/2\), in which case use the buffered
conservative acceptance event from Theorem~E.  For \(p_c>1/2\),
\(c_{p_c}=F_{\chi^2_1}^{-1}(2p_c-1)>0\), and the unbuffered exact branch of
Theorem~E applies.  If the population certificate is full rank or
rank-one non-normal, use the exact projected-Gaussian branch.  If it is zero
covariance, use the retained-rank-zero buffered branch.  If it is
inconclusive, no coverage-certified branch is selected.

Generate conditionally independent auxiliary Gaussian draws
\[
Z_{N,m}^{\ast}\sim\mathcal N(0,\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}),
\qquad 1\le m\le R,
\]
where \(R_N\to\infty\) and \(R_N\) is unrelated to the separated-batch count
\(M_N\).  These are plug-in draws from the limiting projected Gaussian law,
not resampled records.  Project each draw onto
\[
H_z=\{h:h_2\le zh_1\},\qquad
\Pi_{H_z}(u)
=u-\frac{(u_2-zu_1)_+}{1+z^2}\binom{-z}{1},
\]
and sort the quadratic values
\[
T_{N,m}^{\ast}(z)
=\Pi_{H_z}(Z_{N,m}^{\ast})^\top
\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}^{+}
\Pi_{H_z}(Z_{N,m}^{\ast}).
\]
The ideal critical value \(c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)\) is the lower conditional
\((1-\alpha)\)-quantile of this projected-Gaussian law.  In the retained
full-rank branch it is constructed from the conditional analogue of
\[
F_{z,\Sigma}(c)=
\PP\{Z\in H_z,\ Z^\top\Sigma^{-1}Z\le c\}
+\PP\{Z\notin H_z,\ 
(\Pi_{H_z}Z)^\top\Sigma^{-1}(\Pi_{H_z}Z)\le c\}
\]
in \Cref{lem:deterministic-full-rank-projected-gaussian-quantile-main}, with
\(\Sigma\) replaced by the retained \(\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}\).  A
coverage-certified report must evaluate this ideal conditional quantile
directly, either analytically or by the deterministic certified enclosure
routine of
\Cref{thm:certified-projected-gaussian-quantile-algorithm-main,lem:certified-deterministic-projected-gaussian-quantile-main}.  That
routine must use a certified Gaussian tail truncation, interval quadrature over
the two projected regions in the display above, and bisection stopping rules
which produce a quantile bracket \((L,U]\) narrow enough to decide the inequality
\[
Q_N(z)\le c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)
\]
or, on a buffered branch, the inequality with
\(c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)+\varepsilon\).  If no such certified ideal-quantile
evaluation is available, the branch certificate alone is insufficient: report
``ideal quantile only; Monte Carlo diagnostic not coverage-certified'' for
this candidate.

When optional Monte Carlo draws are generated, the raw simulation quantile
estimate is the order statistic
\[
\widehat c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N,R}(z)
=T_{N,(\lceil(1-\alpha)R\rceil)}^{\ast}(z),
\]
after sorting increasingly.  It may be displayed as a diagnostic for the ideal
conditional quantile.  To turn finite simulation into an operational
coverage-certified critical value, choose a Monte Carlo error level
\(\delta_N\downarrow0\) with
\[
\varepsilon_{R_N,\delta_N}
=\sqrt{\frac{\log(2/\delta_N)}{2R_N}}<\alpha .
\]
Let \(q_R=\widehat c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N,R_N}(z)\) and define the DKW inflation by
\[
d^{\mathrm{DKW}}_{R_N,\delta_N,N}(z)
:=
\inf\left\{d\ge0:
\widehat F^\ast_{N,R_N}(q_R+d;z)-\varepsilon_{R_N,\delta_N}
\ge 1-\alpha\right\}.
\]
The DKW one-sided simulated critical value is then
\[
\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R_N,\delta_N}(z)
=T_{N,(\lceil(1-\alpha+\varepsilon_{R_N,\delta_N})R_N\rceil)}^\ast(z),
\]
equivalently
\[
\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R_N,\delta_N}(z)
=q_R+d^{\mathrm{DKW}}_{R_N,\delta_N,N}(z).
\]
The event
\[
G_{\mathrm{MC},N}^{\mathrm{DKW}}(z)
=\{\Delta_{N,R_N}(z)\le\varepsilon_{R_N,\delta_N}\},
\qquad
\Delta_{N,R_N}(z)=
\sup_t|\widehat F^\ast_{N,R_N}(t;z)-F_N^\ast(t;z)|,
\]
has conditional probability at least \(1-\delta_N\), and on it
\[
\widehat c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N,R_N}(z)
+d^{\mathrm{DKW}}_{R_N,\delta_N,N}(z)
\ge c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z).
\]
Equivalently, before conditioning on the auxiliary simulation,
\[
\PP^\ast\{\widehat c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N,R_N}(z)
+d^{\mathrm{DKW}}_{R_N,\delta_N,N}(z)
\ge c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)\}\ge1-\delta_N .
\]
This is the default certified finite-Monte-Carlo choice in the theorem.  The
reported simulated event is then
\[
Q_N(z)\le
\widehat c^{\mathrm{DKW},-}_{1-\alpha,N,R_N,\delta_N}(z),
\]
with coverage transferred from Theorem~E and
\Cref{lem:conditional-monte-carlo-quantile-main}.  Any alternative finite
simulation rule must state its own event \(G_{\mathrm{MC},N}(z)\), error
\(\delta_N\), and one-sided inflation \(d_{R,\delta_N,N}(z)\) satisfying the
same domination condition, and must attach that event to the finite report.
If no such one-sided certificate is available, all simulated quantiles are
diagnostic only: report ``ideal quantile only; Monte Carlo diagnostic not
coverage-certified'' for this candidate.

When several diagonal candidates are displayed, repeat the preceding steps
only for the finite list or finite interval union in \(Z_{\rm cert}\).  Any
uncertified continuum curve, heat map, or dense grid is a visualization of the
diagnostic statistic and is not a confidence-set inversion.
\end{minipage}}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main}]
\Cref{lem:killed-leakage-gap-exponential-moment-main} verifies the
exponential renewal tail.  The stationary CLT for
\(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\) is the standard complete-cycle regenerative
CLT of \Cref{prop:generic-complete-cycle-regenerative-clt-main} specialized in
\Cref{lem:three-inclusion-clt-reduction-main} to the displayed adjacent-gap
reward \(Y_j=U_j-rL_j\).  That specialization supplies Palm centering,
one-gap dependence, endpoint deletion, renewal random-index replacement, and
the covariance scale \(\Sigma_r=T_1^{(r)}/\mu\).  The finite-start transfer
uses the hidden-start path-space total-variation estimate
\Cref{lem:finite-hidden-start-path-contraction-main}; all complete-cycle
indices are formed inside the retained visible word, so no estimator
definition changes after burn-in.

For covariance estimation, use the common complete-cycle set \(J_N\) and the
plug-in rewards \(\widehat Y_j\) in
\Cref{def:cycle-index-inclusion-estimator-main}.  The CLT gives
\(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r=O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\), hence
\(\widehat Y_j-Y_j=-(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r)L_j\).  The hypotheses of
\Cref{thm:finite-record-covariance-main} hold with \(m_{\rm dep}=1\), and
Algorithm~1 gives
\[
\|\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}-\Sigma_r\|_{\operatorname{op}}
=O_{\PP}\left(N^{-1/3}\right),
\]
which is \(o_{\PP}(t_N)\) for \(t_N=N^{-1/4}\).
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \Cref{thm:finite-record-quotient-delta-main}]
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main} gives
\[
\sqrt N(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r)\Rightarrow
\mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r)
\]
and the auxiliary total-space covariance rate for
\(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}\).  The stable quotient hypotheses
\(r_0>0\) and \(D=r_1-r_0^2\ne0\) put the population point in the \(C^1\)
domain of the pre-diagonal quotient rule
\Cref{prop:pre-diagonal-quotient-delta-before-main}.  Applying that
proposition with \(\widehat r_N=\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\) gives the
displayed Gaussian limit and the sandwich covariance formula for
\(\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})\).  This is an ordinary quotient delta
method statement; no exchange-diagonal projected-Gaussian theorem is used.

For the constrained D-MAP population, the deterministic quotient inverse
identity in \Cref{thm:toeplitz_image_inverse_duality} gives
\(\Phi(r)=(p+s,ps)\).  If
\(\Delta_\sigma=\sigma_1^2-4\sigma_2>0\), the quadratic
\[
z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2=0
\]
has the two distinct roots \(\{p,s\}\subset(0,1)\).  Therefore the visible
record identifies the unordered survival-factor set and, after applying
\(z\mapsto-\log z/\Delta\tau\) to each element, the unordered numerical rate
set.  The separated-root branch thresholds in
\Cref{prop:finite-record-quotient-projection-reporting-main} are eventually
retained on any compact separated-root neighbourhood, but the proof stops at
unordered roots: a visible record supplies no ordering of the two hidden
labels.  Ordered labelled-rate Wald reporting is therefore left to the separate
side-data convention \Cref{conv:branch-map-labelled-rates-main} and the
side-data corollary, not to Theorem~D.
\end{proof}

\begin{proof}[Proof of \Cref{thm:finite-record-exchange-diagonal-main}]
The half-space limit is the local-reach projection limit in
\Cref{lem:exchange-diagonal-local-reach-before-main} applied to the
raw symmetric-coordinate CLT and covariance consistency derived in
\Cref{lem:dmap-diagonal-raw-quotient-clt-main} from Theorem~C and
\Cref{prop:pre-diagonal-quotient-delta-before-main}.
The retained-rank conditional projected-Gaussian calibration is the general
half-space result of \Cref{lem:projected-gaussian-bootstrap-halfspace-main}.
The actual D-MAP diagonal covariance is one of the alternatives in
\Cref{lem:dmap-diagonal-covariance-alternatives-main}.  In the full-rank and
rank-one non-normal alternatives,
\Cref{thm:projected-gaussian-continuity-degeneracy-main} makes the limiting
projected statistic continuous, so
\Cref{lem:projected-gaussian-bootstrap-halfspace-main} gives convergence of
the lower conditional critical value and exact unbuffered coverage.  In the
rank-one-normal alternative, the same degeneracy lemma gives, with
\(p_c=1-\alpha\),
\[
\PP(T_z=0)=\frac12,\qquad
\PP(T_z\le t)=\frac12+\frac12F_{\chi^2_1}(t),\quad t>0.
\]
Thus \(c_{p_c}=0\) if \(p_c\le1/2\), while
\(c_{p_c}=F_{\chi^2_1}^{-1}(2p_c-1)>0\) if \(p_c>1/2\).  The latter is a
continuity point with exact mass \(p_c=1-\alpha\), so it is covered
by the exact branch; the former is the atom-at-zero branch and uses the
buffered random-quantile lemma.  In the zero-covariance alternative, covariance
consistency and the threshold \(t_N\) force retained rank zero with probability
tending to one, hence the pseudoinverse and \(Q_N(z)\) vanish on that event.
For the rank-one-normal zero-quantile branch and the zero-covariance branch,
the conditional weak convergence of the plug-in projected-Gaussian law supplies
the hypotheses of \Cref{lem:buffered-random-quantile-coverage-main}.  With
\(T_N=Q_N(z)\), \(T=T_z\), \(F_N^\ast(t)=\PP^\ast\{T_N^\ast(z)\le t\}\), and
\(p=1-\alpha\), that lemma gives the fixed-positive-buffer \(\liminf\)
coverage directly, without a continuity assumption at the zero quantile and
without asserting unbuffered exact equality there.  The ideal coverage proof
uses the ideal conditional quantile \(c^{-}_{1-\alpha,N}(z)\).  The finite
Monte Carlo add-on is only the domination argument in
\Cref{lem:conditional-monte-carlo-quantile-main}: on the record-level
certification event \(G_{\mathrm{cert},N}(z)\), the DKW-inflated simulated
critical value contains the ideal one with conditional probability at least
\(1-\delta_N\), so the simulated acceptance event loses at most
\(\delta_N+\PP(G_{\mathrm{cert},N}(z)^c)+o(1)\) relative to the ideal event.  The
push-forward confidence
set and the equality of coverage events are precisely
\Cref{cor:exchange-diagonal-common-rate-confidence-main}.  The separated-root
chart is excluded because on the diagonal the root map has denominator
\(p-s=0\), although the common diagonal numerical rate remains identified.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Inverse delta method and projected exchange-diagonal charts.}
The next propositions keep three operations distinct.  First, the population
quotient inverse is the map \(r\mapsto(p+s,ps)\).  Second, separated labelled
rates require \(\Delta_\sigma>0\) and the deterministic oracle branch map of
\Cref{conv:branch-map-labelled-rates-main}, or an estimated map with the
high-probability oracle-agreement condition stated there.  Third, a
finite-record implementation uses explicit denominator thresholds, projection
onto the feasible symmetric-coordinate set, and a no-label rule when the
empirical roots are not retained.

\begin{lemma}[Feasible symmetric-coordinate set and computable projection]\label{lem:feasible-set-computable-projection-main}
Let
\[
F=\{(x+y,xy):0\le x,y\le1\}.
\]
For \(0\le t\le2\), set
\[
\ell_F(t)=
\begin{cases}
0,&0\le t\le1,\\
t-1,&1\le t\le2 .
\end{cases}
\]
Then
\begin{equation}\label{eq:feasible-symmetric-coordinate-set-main}
F=\{(\sigma_1,\sigma_2):0\le\sigma_1\le2,\quad
\ell_F(\sigma_1)\le\sigma_2\le\sigma_1^2/4\}.
\end{equation}
For any \(v=(v_1,v_2)\in\mathbb R^2\), define
\[
c_v(t)
=\min\{\max\{v_2,\ell_F(t)\},\,t^2/4\},
\qquad 0\le t\le2,
\]
and
\[
q_v(t)=(t-v_1)^2+(c_v(t)-v_2)^2 .
\]
The deterministic projection used below is the one-dimensional rule
\begin{equation}\label{eq:computable-projection-rule-main}
t_v=\min\operatorname*{arg\,min}_{0\le t\le2}q_v(t),
\qquad
\Pi_F^{\rm tb}(v)=(t_v,c_v(t_v)).
\end{equation}
This rule is Borel measurable, is a Euclidean nearest-point projection onto
\(F\), and resolves global nonuniqueness by choosing the smallest minimizing
\(\sigma_1\).  On any neighbourhood where the Euclidean nearest point is
unique, \(\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\) agrees with the ordinary projection.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Fix \(t=x+y\).  The unit-square constraints imply \(0\le t\le2\).  Among
points in \([0,1]^2\) with sum \(t\), the product \(xy\) is maximized at
\(x=y=t/2\), giving \(xy\le t^2/4\).  The minimum is attained at the boundary:
it is \(0\) for \(0\le t\le1\), and \(t-1\) for \(1\le t\le2\), because then
one coordinate can be \(1\) and the other \(t-1\).  Conversely, every value
between these two endpoints is obtained by continuously moving \(x\) along the
line segment \(\{(x,t-x):0\le x,t-x\le1\}\).  This proves
\eqref{eq:feasible-symmetric-coordinate-set-main}.

For fixed first coordinate \(t\), the closest feasible second coordinate to
\(v_2\) is exactly the clipping \(c_v(t)\) to the closed interval
\([\ell_F(t),t^2/4]\).  Therefore minimizing the squared Euclidean distance
from \(v\) to \(F\) reduces to minimizing \(q_v\) over the compact interval
\([0,2]\).  The minimum exists because \(q_v\) is continuous.  Choosing the
smallest minimizer gives the displayed deterministic tie-break.  Since the
argmin graph of a continuous function on a compact metric space is closed, the
leftmost argmin selection is Borel measurable.  The selected point is a nearest
point by the preceding fixed-\(t\) reduction, and uniqueness makes the
tie-break irrelevant.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Fixed-window error bound for projected renewal reports]\label{lem:projected-complete-law-fixed-window-error-main}
Let
\[
F_{\rm sep}^{\circ}
=\{(x+y,xy):0<x<1,\ 0<y<1,\ x\ne y\}.
\]
Fix a compact set \(K\Subset F_{\rm sep}^{\circ}\).  For
\(\sigma\in K\), let \(\PP_\sigma\) denote the stationary renewal law
obtained by the projected complete-law reconstruction of
\Cref{lem:algorithmic-complete-law-reconstruction-main}: compute the two
unordered roots of \(z^2-\sigma_1z+\sigma_2=0\), form the corresponding
gap law \(g_k(\sigma)\), set
\(\rho_*(\sigma)=\{1+\sum_{k\ge0}k g_k(\sigma)\}^{-1}\), and then use the
renewal inclusion product and finite inclusion--exclusion.

For every finite window \(I\subset\mathbb Z\) there is a finite constant
\(C_I(K)\) such that, for all \(\sigma,\sigma'\in K\),
\begin{equation}\label{eq:projected-complete-law-fixed-window-lipschitz-main}
\max_{S\subset I}
\left|\PP_\sigma\{\varXi\cap I=S\}
      -\PP_{\sigma'}\{\varXi\cap I=S\}\right|
\le C_I(K)\,\|\sigma-\sigma'\| .
\end{equation}
Consequently, if the population point \(\sigma_0=\Phi(r)\) lies in
\(F_{\rm sep}^{\circ}\) and the finite-record projected coordinate
\(\widetilde\sigma_N\) is retained in such a compact \(K\) with
\(\sigma_0\in K\), then the reported projected complete-law probabilities
satisfy the fixed-window deterministic bound
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-record-projected-word-error-bound-main}
\max_{S\subset I}
\left|\widetilde\PP_N\{\varXi\cap I=S\}
      -\PP_{\sigma_0}\{\varXi\cap I=S\}\right|
\le C_I(K)\,\|\widetilde\sigma_N-\sigma_0\|.
\end{equation}
On a denominator-stable neighbourhood of \(r\), if projection is inactive and
\(\Phi\) has Lipschitz constant \(L_\Phi\), then the same event also gives
\[
\max_{S\subset I}
\left|\widetilde\PP_N\{\varXi\cap I=S\}
      -\PP_{\sigma_0}\{\varXi\cap I=S\}\right|
\le C_I(K)L_\Phi\,\|\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r\|.
\]
In particular, under the finite-record CLT hypotheses of
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main}, every fixed-window projected
complete-law report has error \(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\) at separated interior
population points.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
Compactness in \(F_{\rm sep}^{\circ}\) gives constants \(\eta>0\) and
\(\delta>0\) such that every root pair over \(K\) lies in
\([\eta,1-\eta]^2\) and has separation at least \(\delta\).  Therefore the
ordered roots
\[
z_-(\sigma)=\frac{\sigma_1-\sqrt{\sigma_1^2-4\sigma_2}}2,\qquad
z_+(\sigma)=\frac{\sigma_1+\sqrt{\sigma_1^2-4\sigma_2}}2
\]
are \(C^1\), with uniformly bounded derivatives on a neighbourhood of \(K\).
The logarithms \(\theta_\pm(\sigma)=-\log z_\pm(\sigma)\) are also \(C^1\)
there.  For each fixed \(k\), the unequal-root formula in
\eqref{eq:algorithmic-reconstruction-gap-main} is therefore \(C^1\) in
\(\sigma\) on that neighbourhood.

The derivative bounds are uniform for the infinite mean appearing in
\(\rho_*\).  Indeed \(z_\pm\le1-\eta\) on a neighbourhood of \(K\), and the
derivatives of \(z_\pm^k\) are bounded by a constant times
\((k+1)(1-\eta/2)^k\) after shrinking the neighbourhood.  Hence
\(\sum_{k\ge0} k g_k(\sigma)\) and its first derivatives converge uniformly
on \(K\).  Thus \(\rho_*(\sigma)\) is Lipschitz on \(K\).

If \(I=\emptyset\), the only word has probability \(1\) under every
\(\PP_\sigma\), so the claim is trivial.  Otherwise let
\(d_I=\max I-\min I\), with \(d_I=0\) for a one-point window.  The Palm
renewal mass \(u_n(\sigma)\), \(0\le n\le d_I\), is a finite polynomial in
\(g_0(\sigma),\ldots,g_{n-1}(\sigma)\), with \(u_0=1\).  Hence each
\(u_n\) needed by the inclusion products in the window is Lipschitz on
\(K\).  For a nonempty \(B=\{i_1<\cdots<i_m\}\subset I\), the inclusion
functional
\[
\rho_*(\sigma)\prod_{\ell=1}^{m-1}
u_{i_{\ell+1}-i_\ell}(\sigma)
\]
is therefore Lipschitz on \(K\), with a constant depending only on \(I\) and
\(K\).  The empty inclusion has constant value \(1\).  Finally, each word
probability is a finite signed sum of at most \(2^{|I|}\) such inclusion
functionals, by
\eqref{eq:algorithmic-reconstruction-word-main}.  Taking the maximum over the
finite set of \(S\subset I\) gives
\eqref{eq:projected-complete-law-fixed-window-lipschitz-main}.

The finite-record statement is obtained by substituting
\(\sigma'=\widetilde\sigma_N\) and \(\sigma=\sigma_0\) in
\eqref{eq:projected-complete-law-fixed-window-lipschitz-main}; the reported
\(\widetilde\PP_N\) is exactly \(\PP_{\widetilde\sigma_N}\) on the retained
complete-law branch.  On a denominator-stable neighbourhood the map
\(\Phi\) is \(C^1\), hence Lipschitz.  When projection is inactive,
\(\widetilde\sigma_N=\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})\), and the final
display follows.  The \(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\) conclusion follows from
\(\sqrt N(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}-r)=O_{\PP}(1)\) in
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main} and from the fact that
projection is eventually inactive with probability tending to one at
separated interior points.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Finite-record projected complete-law theorem]\label{prop:finite-record-complete-law-renewal-consistency-main}
Let \(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\) be the complete-cycle inclusion estimator,
let \(\Phi\) be the quotient inverse map, and let
\(\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\) be the feasible symmetric-coordinate projection.  Use the
finite-record reporting gate \(\mathcal E_N\) of
\eqref{eq:finite-record-reporting-event-main} and the deterministic thresholds
\(\varepsilon_N,\eta_N\) in \eqref{prot:finite-record-main}.  The projected
complete-law report is the following partial finite-record procedure.  On
\(\mathcal E_N\), define
\[
\widetilde\sigma_N
=\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\{\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})\},
\]
let \(\{ \widetilde z_{1,N},\widetilde z_{2,N}\}\) be the unordered roots of
\[
z^2-\widetilde\sigma_{1,N}z+\widetilde\sigma_{2,N}=0,
\]
and set
\begin{equation}\label{eq:complete-law-retained-event-main}
\mathcal E_N^{\rm law}
:=\mathcal E_N\cap
\{\widetilde z_{1,N},\widetilde z_{2,N}\in[\eta_N,1-\eta_N]\}.
\end{equation}
On \((\mathcal E_N^{\rm law})^c\), no projected complete-law probabilities and
no finite word probabilities are formed.  On \(\mathcal E_N^{\rm law}\), the
projected gap law
\(\widetilde g_{k,N}\) computed from
\Cref{lem:algorithmic-complete-law-reconstruction-main} is nonnegative,
normalized, and is the \(D\)-row path law of a stochastic triangular kernel:
\[
\widetilde g_{k,N}\ge0,\qquad
\sum_{k\ge0}\widetilde g_{k,N}=1 .
\]
Only on this same retained event define
\[
\widetilde\rho_N
=\rho_*(\widetilde\sigma_N)
=\left(1+\sum_{k\ge0}k\,\widetilde g_{k,N}\right)^{-1},
\]
let \(\widetilde u_{n,N}\) be the Palm renewal masses of this same projected
gap law, and define the finite-record law \(\widetilde\PP_N\) by
\begin{equation}\label{eq:finite-record-renewal-consistent-inclusions-main}
\widetilde\PP_N(B\subset\varXi)
=\widetilde\rho_N
 \prod_{\ell=1}^{m-1}\widetilde u_{i_{\ell+1}-i_\ell,N},
\qquad B=\{i_1<\cdots<i_m\}\ne\emptyset,
\end{equation}
with empty-set inclusion value \(1\), and then by finite inclusion--exclusion
for words.  This law is an exact stationary renewal law.  Equivalently, on
\(\mathcal E_N^{\rm law}\),
\[
(\widetilde\rho_N,\widetilde\sigma_N)\in
\mathcal M_{\rm ren}
:=\{(\rho_*(\sigma),\sigma):\sigma\in F\}.
\]
The empirical coordinate \(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\) is used only to
construct the quotient coordinate and to test denominator stability; after
projection, the renewal intensity is \(\widetilde\rho_N\), not
\(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\).

Assume now that the population point is separated and interior:
\(\Phi(r)\in F_{\rm sep}^{\circ}\), with the inverse denominators bounded
away from zero.  If
\(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}}\to r\) in probability, then the projected
coordinate satisfies \(\widetilde\sigma_N\to\Phi(r)\) and
\(\widetilde\rho_N\to r_0\) in probability on the retained branch, whose
probability tends to one for any thresholds tending to zero slowly enough.
For every fixed finite window \(I\) there are a compact separated-root
neighbourhood \(K\) of \(\Phi(r)\) and a constant \(C_I(K)<\infty\) such that,
with probability tending to one,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:complete-law-reporting-fixed-window-bound-main}
\max_{S\subset I}
\left|\widetilde\PP_N\{\varXi\cap I=S\}
      -\PP\{\varXi\cap I=S\}\right|
\le C_I(K)\,\|\widetilde\sigma_N-\Phi(r)\|.
\end{equation}
If the finite-record CLT hypotheses of
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main} hold, the right-hand side is
\(O_{\PP}(N^{-1/2})\).  Thus each fixed-window retained projected word
probability converges in probability to its population value with an explicit
projection-coordinate error bound.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
On \((\mathcal E_N^{\rm law})^c\) the proposition asserts only the prescribed
withholding rule, so no renewal law or word-probability report has to be
interpreted there.  On \(\mathcal E_N^{\rm law}\), the reporting gate has passed, the
quotient inverse has been evaluated, and the construction first projects the
symmetric root coordinate into \(F\).  Since the projected roots lie in
\([\eta_N,1-\eta_N]\subset(0,1)\),
\Cref{lem:projected-root-gap-law-valid-main} applies to those roots and shows
that the quotient-root formulas are \(D\)-row path probabilities of a
stochastic triangular labelled kernel.  They therefore give a nonnegative
unit-mass gap law, hence a single Palm renewal law.  The intensity
\(\widetilde\rho_N\) is then defined from that same gap law by the renewal
identity \(1/\EE(G+1)\).  Therefore
\((\widetilde\rho_N,\widetilde\sigma_N)\) lies on \(\mathcal M_{\rm ren}\),
and the inclusion products are exactly the renewal products for the projected
finite-record renewal law.

At a separated interior population point, the inverse denominators are bounded
away from zero and \(\Phi(r)\) lies in the relative interior of \(F\).  The
complete-cycle inclusion estimator is consistent by
\Cref{thm:finite-record-inference-audit-main}; hence
\(\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})\to\Phi(r)\) in probability and
\(\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\) is eventually the identity with probability tending to
one.  The denominator thresholds in \(\mathcal E_N\) pass with probability
tending to one by \Cref{lem:finite-record-reporting-gate-main}.  The two
population roots lie in \((0,1)\), while \(\eta_N\downarrow0\), so the
interior-root test in \(\mathcal E_N^{\rm law}\) also passes with probability
tending to one.  Thus the conditional law-reporting event itself satisfies
\[
\PP\{(\mathcal E_N^{\rm law})^c\}\to0 .
\]
The
roots, gap probabilities for any fixed finite range, renewal masses for any
fixed finite range, and \(\rho_*(\sigma)\) are continuous on this separated
interior chart.  The quantitative fixed-window statement is exactly
\Cref{lem:projected-complete-law-fixed-window-error-main}, applied on a compact
separated-root neighbourhood of \(\Phi(r)\).  The displayed fixed-window
convergence follows from that bound.
\end{proof}

\begin{remark}[Implementation note for finite-record complete-law reporting]\label{rem:finite-record-complete-law-implementation-main}
The operational reporting rule is the following finite-record projection
procedure.  Use the finite-record reporting gate \(\mathcal E_N\), the raw
inverse map \(\Phi\), the projection \(\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\), and the thresholds of
\Cref{prop:finite-record-quotient-projection-reporting-main}.  Equivalently,
this is exactly the retained event \(\mathcal E_N^{\rm law}\) in
\eqref{eq:complete-law-retained-event-main}.  A complete-law report is formed
only on \(\mathcal E_N\) and only when
\[
\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}\ge\varepsilon_N,\qquad
|\widehat r_{1,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}}
-(\widehat r_{0,N}^{\mathrm{cyc}})^2|\ge\varepsilon_N .
\]
On that event compute
\[
\widetilde\sigma_N
=\Pi_F^{\rm tb}\{\Phi(\widehat r_N^{\mathrm{cyc}})\},
\]
then compute the projected roots of
\[
z^2-\widetilde\sigma_{1,N}z+\widetilde\sigma_{2,N}=0 .
\]
Withhold complete-law probabilities if either projected root lies outside
\([\eta_N,1-\eta_N]\).  On the retained interior-root branch, use
\Cref{prop:finite-record-complete-law-renewal-consistency-main} for the
renewal law and use
\Cref{rem:complete-law-numerical-implementation-main} for finite-window
coefficient evaluation and certified truncation of the scalar mean.  If the
reporting gate, denominator test, projection-root test, or declared numerical
tolerance certificate fails, no finite-record complete-law probabilities are
reported.
\end{remark}

\begin{proposition}[Symmetric-coordinate inverse delta method]\label{cor:finite_inverse_delta_boundary_main}\label{cor:finite-separated-root-rate-chart-main}
Work under \Cref{ass:fixed-sampled-boundary-record-main}, and let
\(\widehat r_N\) be the all-complete-cycle inclusion estimator from
\Cref{def:cycle-index-inclusion-estimator-main}.  Suppose
\[
\sqrt N(\widehat r_N-r)\Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_r)
\]
with a reported covariance estimator \(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}\) whose auxiliary
total-space completion \(\widehat\Sigma_{r,N}^{\rm tot}\) satisfies
the rate conclusion of \Cref{thm:finite-record-covariance-main}.  Assume the
population point lies in a stability neighbourhood where \(r_0\) and
\(|r_1/r_0-r_0|\) are bounded away from zero.  With \(\Phi\),
\(\widetilde\sigma_N\), and \(\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}\) defined by
\Cref{prop:finite-record-quotient-projection-reporting-main},
\[
\sqrt N(\Phi(\widehat r_N)-\Phi(r))
\Rightarrow \mathcal N(0,\Sigma_{\sigma}),
\qquad
\Sigma_{\sigma}=D\Phi(r)\Sigma_rD\Phi(r)^\top .
\]
The same limit holds with \(\Phi(\widehat r_N)\) replaced by
\(\widetilde\sigma_N\) at separated interior points of \(F\), and the sandwich
\(\widehat\Sigma_{\sigma,N}\) is consistent there.  For the constrained D-MAP
population, \(\Phi(r)=(p+s,ps)\).  If \(\Delta_\sigma=(p-s)^2>0\), the roots are
recovered as an unordered pair and the finite-record rule may report the
unordered numerical rate set.  The ordered labelled-rate chart is not part of
this proposition; it requires the separate side-data hypotheses in
\Cref{cor:side-data-labelled-rate-wald-main}.
If \(\Delta_\sigma=0\), this proposition asserts only the symmetric-coordinate
delta method.  The diagonal no-label conclusion is the post-Theorem~E
corollary \Cref{cor:post-diagonal-no-label-reporting-main}, and the
common-rate estimand is handled by the projected exchange-diagonal statement.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
On the stated inverse stability neighbourhood, \(\Phi\) is \(C^1\).  The delta
method gives the displayed Gaussian limit for \(\Phi(\widehat r_N)\).  The
sandwich covariance inherits the same operator-norm rate because
\(D\Phi(\widehat r_N)\) is bounded and converges in probability to \(D\Phi(r)\)
on the denominator-retained event.  The identity \(\Phi(r)=(p+s,ps)\) is
exactly the quotient inverse calculation in
\Cref{thm:toeplitz_image_inverse_duality}.  At separated roots,
\(\Phi(r)\) lies in the relative interior of \(F\), so
\Cref{prop:finite-record-quotient-projection-reporting-main} makes projection
asymptotically inactive.  Since
\(\widehat\Delta_{\sigma,N}\to\Delta_\sigma>0\) and \(\delta_N^2\to0\), the
discriminant threshold is eventually passed.  The empirical projected roots
converge to the two distinct population roots in \((0,1)\), so the endpoint
threshold is eventually passed, but the proof stops at the unordered root set
because no statistic of the visible record chooses the branch label.  At
\(\Delta_\sigma=0\), the proof stops at the symmetric-coordinate delta method;
the threshold/no-label statement uses the projected-coordinate limit from
